# TESTING — Descent 3 Testing Plan

Consolidated from the former `plans/` directory. Each part is kept verbatim from its source document; only the parts here are canonical.


---

<a name="part1"></a>
# PART I — OVERVIEW & WORKFLOW

This is the consolidated testing plan for Descent 3, merged from:

- `plans/graphics_testing.md` — Graphics testing master plan (tested/untested matrix, phases, llvmpipe/CI strategy)
- `plans/render_tests.md` — Render test framework plan (tracer, runner, reports; implementation complete)
- `plans/COVERAGE_PLAN.txt` — Unit test coverage checklist
- `plans/render_testing_plan.txt` — Headless render test setup instructions
- `plans/3d_scene_latex_whitepaper.md` — Whitepaper spec for render-test scene documentation

**Build & run commands** are maintained in [`AGENTS.md`](AGENTS.md): `npm run tests` (all), `npm run render-tests` (render only), `npm run unit-tests` (unit only), with `--build-dir`, `--output-dir`, `--no-build`, `--verbose`, `--tracing`, `--serve` flags.

**Current render-test behavior:** each gtest renders one or more PNGs to `build/tests/render_output`; a test fails only if it crashes or errors. Review images in `render_report.html` (self-contained HTMX page: filter, search, click-to-zoom). The combined `tests_report.html` (Unit + Render tabs) is generated by `npm run tests` via `tests/report-tools-ts`.

**Test suites:**
- `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — 44 D3-stack render tests (textures, lighting, alpha, fog, primitives, clipping)
- `tests/cpp_src/` — unit test sources (see Part IV coverage checklist)
- `tests/tracer/` — `-finstrument-functions` tracing for call graphs (opt-in via `--tracing`)

## Architecture References

- **Descent 3 Fusion Engine** — dual engine design: indoor "room" portal renderer + outdoor terrain LOD renderer (SIGGRAPH '96 continuous LOD height fields). Postmortem: [Game Developer, Oct 1999](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/programming/postmortem-outrage-s-descent-3)
- **Renderer Refactor PR #315** — Edu García (Arcnor) moved non-OpenGL code to base class, abstracted triangle rendering. The vision: enable multiple renderers and dynamic API switching. [PR #315](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/pull/315)
- **FBO Render-to-Texture PR #437** — game renders to OpenGL FBO at game resolution, then blits. Enables content-scale handling and consistent output. [Release notes](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/commit/0388d0758298d6e4ddda266062a96d76493c44)
- **Software Renderer Removal PRs #305, #321** — dead software renderer code removed; `USE_SOFTWARE_TNL` macro remains
- **Descent 3 Internals Wiki** — BOA (Potentially Visible Set), HOG2 archive format, Osiris scripting. [Wiki](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/wiki/Descent-3-Internals)

## Renderer Module Layout

| Module | Purpose |
|--------|---------|
| `HardwareOpenGL` | Production OpenGL 3.2 Core renderer (SDL3, FBO, GLSL shaders) |
| `MesaOpenGL` | Mesa llvmpipe headless renderer (EGL pbuffer, fixed-function) |
| `HardwareBaseGPU` | Shared GPU state: alpha blending, bitmap drawing, stats |
| `HardwareDraw` | g3_* drawing: polygons, lines, spheres, bitmaps, boxes |
| `HardwareSetup` | Frame setup, projection matrices, g3_StartFrame/g3_EndFrame |
| `HardwareTransforms` | MVP matrices, viewport/projection/modelview |
| `HardwarePoints` | Point rotation, projection, clipping codes |
| `HardwareClipper` | Polygon/line clipping, temp point management (MAX_POINTS_IN_POLY=100 pool) |
| `HardwareInstance` | Matrix/angle instancing (depth 30 stack) |
| `ShaderProgram` | Templated GLSL VBO/VAO with orphaning (65536 vertex capacity) |
| `dyna_gl.h` | Dynamic GL function loader via SDL_GL_GetProcAddress |

## Shader Pipeline

**Vertex Shader Attributes:**
| Index | Size | Name (GLSL) | C++ Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 3 | `in_pos` | `PosColorUV2Vertex::pos` |
| 1 | 4 | `in_color` | `PosColorUV2Vertex::color` |
| 2 | 2 | `in_uv0` | `PosColorUV2Vertex::uv0` |
| 3 | 2 | `in_uv1` | `PosColorUV2Vertex::uv1` |

**Fragment Shader Uniforms:** `u_texture0`, `u_texture1`, `u_texture_enable` (bitfield), `u_fog_enable`, `u_fog_color`, `u_fog_start`, `u_fog_end`, `u_gamma`

**Fog computation:** `fog_factor = clamp((u_fog_end - distance) / (u_fog_end - u_fog_start), 0, 1)`, then `out_color * fog_factor + (1 - fog_factor) * u_fog_color`

## Test Infrastructure Details

**Full framework spec:** [`plans/render_tests.md`](plans/render_tests.md) — tracer, HTML visualization, TypeScript migration, consolidated report, trace filtering.

**Initialization order (strict, one-time):**
1. `OffscreenGL::Init(800, 600)` — EGL pbuffer + llvmpipe (env: `GALLIUM_DRIVER=llvmpipe`, `MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE=3.3`)
2. `D3Core_Initialize(D3_INIT_MEM | D3_INIT_BITMAP)`
3. `rend_Init(RENDERER_MESA, nullptr, &pref)`
4. `D3Core_Initialize(D3_INIT_GRTEXT)`
5. `AddSearchPath()` — for HOG file discovery

**llvmpipe accuracy:** Mesa llvmpipe correctly executes GLSL shader logic (LLVM JIT), catching most algorithmic bugs. However, it does NOT replicate hardware-specific precision quirks, texture sampling edge cases, or driver optimizations. Sufficient for functional testing and regression detection, but supplement with periodic real-hardware runs (GitHub Actions GPU runners or self-hosted). See [llvmpipe Limitations and CI Strategy](#llvmpipe-limitations-and-ci-strategy).

**State isolation:** `D3RenderTestBase::SetUp()` must aggressively reset ALL GL state (blend, depth, scissor, viewport, bound buffers, active texture unit, shader program) to prevent test ordering dependencies — a common legacy engine porting bug.

**Test base class:** `D3RenderTestBase` provides `BeginFrame()`, `EndFrame()`, `SavePNG()`, `ReadPixels()`, `framebuffer_` (RGBA, Y-flipped), font/text helpers.

**Build & run commands:** [`AGENTS.md`](AGENTS.md) documents the full workflow: `npm run tests` (all tests), `npm run render-tests` (render only), `npm run unit-tests` (unit only), with `--build-dir`, `--output-dir`, `--update-baseline`, `--tracing`, `--serve` flags.

**Game suite pattern:** Overrides `BeginFrame()` to call `mesa_SetLogicalSize()` + `g3_StartFrame()`, and `EndFrame()` to call `g3_EndFrame()`.

**MD5 baselines:** `SavePNG()` writes `{name}.png` via stbi_write_png and `{name}.md5` (32 hex chars) from raw RGBA framebuffer. Run with `--update-baseline` to establish, then compare on subsequent runs.

**MD5 is brittle** — any single pixel change (driver version, Mesa update) breaks the test. Plan to migrate to **SSIM + pixel-diff hybrid** (see Phase 6). Current MD5 approach works for deterministic tests (clears, raw GL quads) but will cause false positives on fuzzier tests (fog, alpha blends, mipmaps).

**Build:** Each suite produces a separate executable linked with `-finstrument-functions` for tracing. All link: `d3_render_test_base`, `mesaopengl`, `descent3` (whole-archive), `tracer`, `GTest::gtest`, `GL`, `GLU`, `EGL`, `PNG`, `SDL3`.

## Reference Data

### Alpha Type Reference

| Alpha Type | Value | Blend Function | GL_BLEND? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `AT_ALWAYS` | 0 | `GL_ONE, GL_ZERO` | No |
| `AT_CONSTANT` | 1 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` | Yes |
| `AT_TEXTURE` | 2 | `GL_ONE, GL_ZERO` | No |
| `AT_CONSTANT_TEXTURE` | 3 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` | Yes |
| `AT_VERTEX` | 4 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` | Yes |
| `AT_CONSTANT_VERTEX` | 5 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` | Yes |
| `AT_TEXTURE_VERTEX` | 6 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` | Yes |
| `AT_CONSTANT_TEXTURE_VERTEX` | 7 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` | Yes |
| `AT_LIGHTMAP_BLEND` | 8 | `GL_DST_COLOR, GL_ZERO` | Yes |
| `AT_SATURATE_TEXTURE` | 9 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE` | Yes |
| `AT_SATURATE_VERTEX` | 12 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE` | Yes |
| `AT_SATURATE_CONSTANT_VERTEX` | 13 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE` | Yes |
| `AT_SATURATE_TEXTURE_VERTEX` | 14 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE` | Yes |
| `AT_SPECULAR` | 32 | (no change) | Yes |
| `AT_LIGHTMAP_BLEND_SATURATE` | 33 | `GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE` | Yes |

### Clipping Codes Reference

`CC_OFF_LEFT=1`, `CC_OFF_RIGHT=2`, `CC_OFF_BOT=4`, `CC_OFF_TOP=8`, `CC_OFF_FAR=16`, `CC_OFF_CUSTOM=32`, `CC_BEHIND=128`

Point coding: `CC_OFF_RIGHT` if `p3_x > p3_z`, `CC_OFF_LEFT` if `p3_x < -p3_z`, `CC_OFF_TOP` if `p3_y > p3_z`, `CC_OFF_BOT` if `p3_y < -p3_z`, `CC_BEHIND` if `p3_z < 0`, `CC_OFF_FAR` if `p3_z > Far_clip_z`

### g3Point Structure

```cpp
struct g3Point {
  float p3_sx, p3_sy;      // Screen x, y (after projection)
  uint8_t p3_codes;          // Clipping codes
  uint8_t p3_flags;          // PF_PROJECTED, PF_FAR_ALPHA, PF_TEMP_POINT, PF_UV, PF_L, PF_RGBA, PF_UV2, PF_ORIGPOINT
  vector p3_vec;             // Rotated x, y, z (use p3_x, p3_y, p3_z)
  vector p3_vecPreRot;       // Original XYZ before rotation
  g3UVL p3_uvl;              // u, v, u2, v2, l/r, g, b, a
};
```

Flags to set on points: `PF_UV` (texture coords), `PF_L` (intensity lighting), `PF_RGBA` (per-vertex RGBA), `PF_UV2` (lightmap coords)

## Phase 1: Core Rendering Features (High Priority)

These are fundamental features used by almost every frame of gameplay. Testing them gives the broadest regression safety.

### Texturing Pipeline

All tests go in `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` (D3 pipeline path). Test class: `D3GameRenderTest` which wraps `BeginFrame()`/`EndFrame()` with `g3_StartFrame()`/`g3_EndFrame()`.

- [ ] **Textured polygon via D3 path**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TexturedPolygonD3`
  - **Setup:** Create a bitmap handle with known pixel data (use `bm_AllocBitmap(64, 64, 0)` or load from HOG). Set pixel pattern: checkerboard or gradient so visual result is verifiable. Call `bm_TransferBitmap(bm_handle, data)` to upload pixel data.
  - **Render flow:**
    1. `BeginFrame()` (calls `g3_StartFrame` with default view at `(0,0,-500)`)
    2. `rend_SetZBufferState(1)`, `rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE)`, `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_ALWAYS)`, `rend_SetAlphaValue(255)`, `rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB)`, `rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(0,0,0))`, `rend_ClearZBuffer()`
    3. Create 4 `g3Point` structs for a quad facing the camera at z=-400
    4. For each point: `g3_RotatePoint(&pt, &world_pos)`, set `p3_flags |= PF_UV | PF_L`, set `p3_u = 0..1`, `p3_v = 0..1`, `p3_l = 1.0f`
    5. `rend_SetTextureType(TT_LINEAR)`
    6. `g3_DrawPoly(4, point_ptrs, bm_handle, MAP_TYPE_BITMAP)`
    7. `EndFrame()`, `ReadPixels()`, `SavePNG("TexturedPolygonD3")`
  - **Verification:** Scan framebuffer for non-black pixels in the expected quad region. Count pixels matching the checkerboard/gradient pattern. At least 80% of expected quad area should have non-background color.

- [ ] **Mipmapping: filter state changes**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `MipmapFiltering`
  - **Setup:** Create a 128x128 bitmap with a distinct high-frequency pattern (alternating rows of red/black). Upload to texture. Create a small quad at z=-200 (close) and a large quad at z=-4000 (far) to exercise different mip levels.
  - **Test A (mip on + bilinear):** `rend_SetMipState(1)`, `rend_SetFiltering(1)`. Render far quad. Expected: blurred/averaged colors (mip filtering active).
  - **Test B (mip off):** `rend_SetMipState(0)`. Render far quad. Expected: sharp aliased colors or different visual artifact.
  - **Test C (mip on, no filtering):** `rend_SetMipState(1)`, `rend_SetFiltering(0)`. Render far quad. Expected: blocky nearest-neighbor look.
  - **Render flow per test:** Same frame setup as above. After `rend_SetMipState()` and `rend_SetFiltering()`, render the quad via `g3_DrawPoly`.
  - **Verification:** Compare pixel variance between mip-on and mip-off renders. Mip-on should have lower variance (more blended colors). Save 3 PNGs: `MipmapFiltering_MipOnBilinear`, `MipmapFiltering_MipOff`, `MipmapFiltering_MipOnNearest`.

- [ ] **Texture wrap modes: REPEAT**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TextureWrapRepeat`
  - **Setup:** Create 32x32 bitmap with a checkerboard pattern. Create a large quad with UV coords from (0,0) to (4,4) — UVs extend beyond [0,1] range.
  - **Render:** `rend_SetWrapType(WT_WRAP)`. Render the quad with `PF_UV` flag.
  - **Verification:** The quad should show the checkerboard pattern repeated 4x4 times. Scan framebuffer: count distinct pattern tiles visible. Expected: 16 tile repetitions.

- [ ] **Texture wrap modes: CLAMP_TO_EDGE**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TextureWrapClamp`
  - **Setup:** Same 32x32 checkerboard bitmap. Same quad with UV (0,0) to (4,4).
  - **Render:** `rend_SetWrapType(WT_CLAMP)`. Render the quad.
  - **Verification:** The quad should show only the center portion of the checkerboard (edge pixels stretched). No repeating pattern. Edge pixels should be uniform in color at the boundaries.

- [ ] **Texture wrap modes: WRAP_V (sky texture mode)**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TextureWrapV`
  - **Setup:** 32x32 gradient bitmap (vertical gradient from red to black). Quad with UV (0,0) to (2,4).
  - **Render:** `rend_SetWrapType(WT_WRAP_V)`. S wraps once (clamped), T wraps 4 times (repeated).
  - **Verification:** Horizontal direction shows single gradient (clamped). Vertical direction shows gradient repeating 4 times.

- [ ] **Bilinear filtering toggle**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `BilinearFilterToggle`
  - **Setup:** Create 8x8 bitmap with 4 colored quadrants (red, green, blue, yellow). Render a 200x200 screen-space quad (scaled up significantly to make filtering visible).
  - **Test A (filtering on):** `rend_SetFiltering(1)`. Expected: smooth gradient at quadrant boundaries.
  - **Test B (filtering off):** `rend_SetFiltering(0)`. Expected: sharp pixelated boundaries between quadrants.
  - **Verification:** For filtering-off, scan a horizontal line across a boundary. Pixel values should jump abruptly. For filtering-on, pixel values should transition gradually. Count transition width in pixels.

- [ ] **Multi-texturing / lightmap blend**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `MultitextureLightmapBlend`
  - **Setup:** Create two bitmaps: (1) base texture with red/green quadrants, (2) lightmap with a radial gradient (white center, black edges). Upload both.
  - **Confirmed vertex format:** `PosColorUV2Vertex` in `HardwareInternal.h` has dual UV layers: `uv0` (primary texture) and `uv1` (lightmap/secondary). Each is a `tex_array` with `(s, t, r, w)` fields.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_LIGHTMAP_BLEND)` — sets blend to `GL_DST_COLOR, GL_ZERO`
    2. `rend_SetOverlayMap(lightmap_handle)`, `rend_SetOverlayType(OT_BLEND)`
    3. Render quad with both textures. Points need `PF_UV | PF_UV2` flags: `p3_u/p3_v` for base texture (uv0), `p3_u2/p3_v2` for lightmap (uv1).
    4. `rend_SetWrapType(WT_CLAMP)` (lightmaps always use WT_CLAMP internally)
  - **Verification:** Center of quad should be bright (lightmap white × base color). Edges should be dark (lightmap black × base color = black). Compare center pixel brightness vs edge pixel brightness. Expected: center > 3x edge brightness.

- [ ] **Multi-texturing: disable lightmap**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `MultitextureNoOverlay`
  - **Setup:** Same two bitmaps as above.
  - **Render:** `rend_SetOverlayType(OT_NONE)`. Render the same quad.
  - **Verification:** Output should show only base texture (no darkening at edges). All pixels should have uniform base color intensity.

- [ ] **Texture cache: LRU eviction**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TextureCacheLRU`
  - **Setup:** Create N bitmaps (where N exceeds cache capacity — check `MAX_BITMAPS` or cache size constant). Upload all with `bm_TransferBitmap`. Render the first one, then the last one.
  - **Render:** For each bitmap: set as current, render a small quad, `rend_ResetCache()` between groups.
  - **Verification:** After rendering all textures, re-render the first one. If LRU eviction occurred, the first texture should have been evicted and re-uploaded. Check that the rendered output matches the expected first texture pattern. Save PNG showing first texture re-rendered after cache fill.

- [ ] **Scaled bitmap rendering**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ScaledBitmap`
  - **Setup:** Create 32x32 bitmap with checkerboard pattern.
  - **Render:** Call `rend_DrawScaledBitmap(100, 100, 300, 300, bm_handle, 0.0f, 0.0f, 1.0f, 1.0f)`. This draws the 32x32 bitmap scaled to 200x200 at screen position (100,100).
  - **Verification:** The 200x200 region should show the checkerboard pattern stretched. Check pixels at corners match expected pattern colors. Non-rect area should be background color.

- [ ] **Chunked bitmap rendering**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ChunkedBitmap`
  - **Setup:** Create a large bitmap (e.g., 512x512). The engine should automatically split it into chunks (check `chunked_bitmap` struct in headers for chunk size, typically 256x256 or similar).
  - **Render:** Populate a `chunked_bitmap` struct with the bitmap handle. Call `rend_DrawChunkedBitmap(&chunk, 50, 50, 255)` (alpha=255 for fully opaque).
  - **Verification:** The full 512x512 image should render without gaps between chunks. Check chunk boundary pixels for seams (should be seamless). Scan for any background-colored pixels within the expected image area — there should be none.

### Lighting Pipeline

- [ ] **LS_FLAT_GOURAUD: flat-colored gouraud polygon**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FlatGouraudLighting`
  - **Render flow:**
    1. `BeginFrame()`, standard clear
    2. `rend_SetLighting(LS_FLAT_GOURAUD)`
    3. `rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(255, 128, 0))` — orange
    4. `rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB)`
    5. Create 4 g3Points for a quad. Set `p3_flags |= PF_UV | PF_L`, `p3_l = 1.0f`
    6. `rend_SetTextureType(TT_FLAT)` (no texture, just color)
    7. `g3_DrawPoly(4, point_ptrs, 0, MAP_TYPE_BITMAP)`
  - **Internal logic:** `DeterminePointColor()` will return `{cur_color.r/255, cur_color.g/255, cur_color.b/255, alpha}` because `LS_FLAT_GOURAUD` forces flat color override.
  - **Verification:** All pixels in the quad should be orange (255, 128, 0). Scan the quad region: count pixels where R≈255, G≈128, B≈0. Expected: >95% of quad pixels match.

- [ ] **LS_GOURAUD: per-vertex color gouraud shading**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `GouraudLightingD3`
  - **Setup:** Create a quad with 4 vertices at different depths (tilted toward camera) to show interpolation.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetLighting(LS_GOURAUD)`
    2. `rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB)`
    3. Create 4 g3Points with different per-vertex colors:
       - Vertex 0: `p3_r=1.0, p3_g=0.0, p3_b=0.0` (red), `p3_flags |= PF_UV | PF_RGBA`
       - Vertex 1: `p3_r=0.0, p3_g=1.0, p3_b=0.0` (green)
       - Vertex 2: `p3_r=0.0, p3_g=0.0, p3_b=1.0` (blue)
       - Vertex 3: `p3_r=1.0, p3_g=1.0, p3_b=1.0` (white)
    4. `rend_SetTextureType(TT_FLAT)`
    5. `g3_DrawPoly(4, point_ptrs, 0, MAP_TYPE_BITMAP)`
  - **Verification:** Colors should smoothly interpolate across the quad. Sample pixel at each corner region — should match the vertex color. Sample center pixel — should be approximately gray (average of all 4 colors). Expected: center pixel R≈128, G≈128, B≈128 (±20 tolerance).

- [ ] **CM_MONO: intensity-based lighting**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `MonoLighting`
  - **Setup:** Same tilted quad as above.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetLighting(LS_GOURAUD)`
    2. `rend_SetColorModel(CM_MONO)`
    3. Set different intensity values per vertex: `p3_l = 0.2, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0`
    4. `p3_flags |= PF_UV | PF_L`
    5. Render with `g3_DrawPoly`
  - **Internal logic:** `DeterminePointColor()` returns `{p3_l, p3_l, p3_l, alpha}` for CM_MONO — grayscale from intensity.
  - **Verification:** Each corner should be grayscale with different brightness. No color tint. Scan corner regions: R≈G≈B at each corner. Center should be medium gray (average intensity ≈ 0.575).

- [ ] **CM_RGB: full per-vertex RGBA**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `RGBALighting`
  - **Already partially tested** in `DepthTwoOverlappingQuads` (sets `p3_r/g/b/a` per vertex). Expand to verify alpha component:
    1. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_VERTEX)`
    2. Set `p3_a = 0.5` on all vertices
    3. Render over a colored background
    4. **Verification:** Polygon should be semi-transparent. Background color should blend through. Compare pixel colors at polygon center vs background: expected blended color.

- [ ] **DeterminePointColor: texture quality = 0 edge case**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `NoTextureQuality`
  - **Concept:** When texture quality is 0, the renderer falls back to flat color even if a texture handle is provided. This is controlled by the game's texture quality setting.
  - **Confirmed signature:** `DeterminePointColor()` in `HardwareInternal.h` takes params: `disableGouraud` (ignore `LS_FLAT_GOURAUD`), `checkTextureQuality` (fallback to flat at quality=0), `flatColorForNoLight` (use flat color when `LS_NONE`).
  - **Render:** Set texture quality to minimum (check how — likely a global variable or `rend_SetFiltering`-related state). Render a textured polygon with `checkTextureQuality=true`.
  - **Verification:** Output should show flat color, not texture. Pixel colors should be uniform across the polygon.

### Alpha & Blending

- [ ] **AT_CONSTANT: constant alpha transparency**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaConstant`
  - **Setup:** Render a red background quad (full opacity). Then render a blue quad over it with constant alpha.
  - **Render:**
    1. First quad: `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_ALWAYS)`, red color, draw full-screen quad
    2. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_CONSTANT)`, `rend_SetAlphaValue(128)` (50% opacity)
    3. Second quad: blue color, draw smaller quad centered on screen
  - **Internal logic:** `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_CONSTANT)` calls `glBlendFunc(GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA)`. Alpha value 128 → 0.5 multiplier.
  - **Verification:** At the overlap region, pixel color should be approximately `(red * 0.5 + blue * 0.5)`. Expected: purple (R≈128, G≈0, B≈128). Non-overlap areas: pure red or pure blue.

- [ ] **AT_TEXTURE: texture alpha channel**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaTexture`
  - **Setup:** Create a 64x64 bitmap with a circular alpha pattern: center pixels have alpha=255, edge pixels have alpha=0, smooth gradient in between.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_TEXTURE)`
    2. Render textured quad over a colored background (green)
    3. `rend_SetTextureType(TT_LINEAR)`
  - **Verification:** Center of texture should be fully opaque (showing texture color). Edges should be transparent (showing green background). Middle ring should show blended colors. Scan radial line from center to edge: alpha should decrease smoothly.

- [ ] **AT_VERTEX: per-vertex alpha**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaVertex`
  - **Setup:** Quad with per-vertex alpha values: corner 0 has `p3_a=1.0`, corner 2 has `p3_a=0.0`, others `p3_a=0.5`.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_VERTEX)`
    2. Set `p3_flags |= PF_UV | PF_RGBA` on all points
    3. Render over red background
  - **Verification:** Corner 0 region: fully opaque (shows vertex color). Corner 2 region: fully transparent (shows red background). Other corners: 50% blend.

- [ ] **AT_CONSTANT_TEXTURE: combined constant × texture alpha**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaConstantTexture`
  - **Setup:** Texture with varying alpha (same as AT_TEXTURE test). Set `rend_SetAlphaValue(128)`.
  - **Render:** `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_CONSTANT_TEXTURE)`. Render textured quad.
  - **Internal logic:** Final alpha = `texture_alpha * (constant_alpha / 255)`. With constant=128, final alpha is halved at every pixel.
  - **Verification:** Compare with AT_TEXTURE test. Every pixel should be more transparent. Center of texture (which was fully opaque) should now be 50% opaque.

- [ ] **AT_LIGHTMAP_BLEND: lightmap blend mode**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaLightmapBlend`
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_LIGHTMAP_BLEND)` → `glBlendFunc(GL_DST_COLOR, GL_ZERO)`
    2. This multiplies source color by destination color (darkening blend)
    3. Render a white quad over a colored background
  - **Verification:** White quad over red background → output is red (white × red = red). White quad over black background → output is black (white × black = black). Gray quad (0.5) over red → output is half-brightness red.

- [ ] **AT_SATURATE_TEXTURE: saturate blending**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaSaturateTexture`
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetAlphaType(AT_SATURATE_TEXTURE)` → `glBlendFunc(GL_SRC_ALPHA, GL_ONE)` (additive)
    2. Render a semi-transparent white quad over a red background
  - **Verification:** Colors should add up (brighten). White (0.5 alpha) over red → result is brighter than red, approaching white. R > background R, G > background G, B > background B.

- [ ] **Alpha factor: global scale**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `AlphaFactorScale`
  - **Setup:** Render a textured quad with `AT_CONSTANT` at alpha=255.
  - **Test A:** `rend_SetAlphaFactor(1.0f)`. Full opacity.
  - **Test B:** `rend_SetAlphaFactor(0.3f)`. Globally scaled down.
  - **Internal logic:** `rend_GetAlphaMultiplier()` returns `cur_alpha/255` for constant types. This is multiplied by `gpu_Alpha_factor` in `DeterminePointColor()`.
  - **Verification:** Test B should be significantly more transparent than Test A. Background should show through more. Compare overlap region pixel alpha.

- [ ] **Transparency overlay: two overlapping semi-transparent polygons**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TransparencyOverlap`
  - **Setup:** Background (blue). Polygon A (red, 50% alpha, left half). Polygon B (green, 50% alpha, right half, overlapping A in center).
  - **Render:** Draw A first, then B (order matters for transparency).
  - **Verification:** Three regions visible: pure red (A only), pure green (B only), blended region (A+B). The overlap should show a composite of red+green over blue background. Verify pixel colors in each region match expected blend formulas.

### Fog

- [ ] **Fog enable/disable**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FogEnableDisable`
  - **Setup:** Render 5 quads at increasing Z depths: z=-200, z=-500, z=-1000, z=-2000, z=-4000. All quads are red.
  - **Test A (fog off):** `rend_SetFogState(0)`. All quads should be pure red.
  - **Test B (fog on):** `rend_SetFogState(1)`, `rend_SetFogBorders(500, 3000)`, `rend_SetFogColor(GR_RGB(200, 200, 200))`.
  - **Shader logic:** `fog_factor = clamp((fog_end - distance) / (fog_end - fog_start), 0, 1)`. At z=-200 (distance < 500): fog_factor=1.0 (no fog). At z=-3000 (distance > 3000): fog_factor=0.0 (full fog).
  - **Verification Test B:**
    - Quad at z=-200: pure red (no fog, within near border)
    - Quad at z=-500: mostly red (at near border, fog_factor ≈ 1.0)
    - Quad at z=-1000: reddish-gray (mid-range, fog_factor ≈ 0.8)
    - Quad at z=-2000: mostly gray (far, fog_factor ≈ 0.4)
    - Quad at z=-4000: pure gray (beyond far border, fog_factor = 0.0)
  - Scan each quad's center pixel. Verify color transitions from red → gray as depth increases.

- [ ] **Fog near/far borders**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FogBorders`
  - **Setup:** Same 5 quads at different depths.
  - **Test A:** `rend_SetFogBorders(200, 500)` — narrow fog band. Most quads should be fully fogged.
  - **Test B:** `rend_SetFogBorders(200, 5000)` — wide fog band. Only the farthest quad should be fully fogged.
  - **Verification:** Compare fog coverage between A and B. Test A: quads at z=-500 and beyond should be near-full fog color. Test B: only z=-4000 and z=-5000 should show significant fog.

- [ ] **Fog color**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FogColor`
  - **Setup:** Single quad at z=-2000 (mid-fog range).
  - **Test A:** `rend_SetFogColor(GR_RGB(255, 0, 0))` — red fog
  - **Test B:** `rend_SetFogColor(GR_RGB(0, 0, 255))` — blue fog
  - **Verification:** Test A: far quad should tint red. Test B: far quad should tint blue. Sample pixel color and check dominant channel.

- [ ] **Fog + textured polygon interaction**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FogWithTexture`
  - **Setup:** Textured quad at z=-2000 (checkered pattern).
  - **Render:** Enable fog with green fog color. Render textured quad.
  - **Verification:** Texture pattern should still be visible but tinted green. The fog color blends with the textured output: `out_color * fog_factor + (1 - fog_factor) * fog_color`. Check that checkerboard pattern is discernible but green-shifted.

- [ ] **Fog: distance-based vs Z-depth**
  - **Rationale:** Legacy OpenGL used `glFogCoordf` or per-vertex fog; D3's shader uses `length(vertex_modelview_pos)` — Euclidean distance from camera, not Z depth.
  - **Test:** Place a polygon at an angle to the camera so different parts have the same Z but different distances. Verify fog intensity varies with distance, not Z. Compare with a polygon parallel to the view plane (same Z, same distance) — fog should be uniform.

## Phase 2: Clipping & Z-Buffer

### Z-Buffer

- [ ] **Z-write mask toggle**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ZBufferWriteMask`
  - **Setup:** Three quads at different depths: back (z=-100, red), middle (z=-200, green), front (z=-300, blue).
  - **Render sequence:**
    1. `rend_SetZBufferState(1)`, `rend_ClearZBuffer()`
    2. Render back (red)
    3. Render middle (green)
    4. `rend_SetZBufferWriteMask(0)` — disable depth writes
    5. Render front (blue)
    6. `rend_SetZBufferWriteMask(1)` — re-enable
    7. Render a new quad at z=-150 (between back and middle), yellow
  - **Verification:** Steps 1-3: green covers red, blue covers green (normal depth test). Step 5: blue quad writes to color buffer but NOT depth buffer. Step 7: yellow quad should be occluded by blue (depth test still reads, but blue's depth wasn't written in step 5, so yellow appears behind blue but in front of green). Expected: yellow visible in the region between green and blue depth planes.

- [ ] **Z-bias: coplanar polygon separation**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ZBiasCoplanar`
  - **Setup:** Two quads at the EXACT same position (z=-300). Quad A: red, rendered first. Quad B: green, rendered second with z-bias.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetZBufferState(1)`, `rend_ClearZBuffer()`
    2. `rend_SetZBias(0.0f)`, render red quad
    3. `rend_SetZBias(5.0f)`, render green quad
  - **Internal logic (confirmed):** `g3_GetModelViewMatrix()` in `HardwareTransforms.cpp` applies Z_bias at `mvMat[14]` (row 3, col 2 — translation Z component). Formula: `mvMat[14] = dot(fvec, pos) + Z_bias`. Positive bias pushes geometry closer to camera (less negative Z in view space → passes depth test). `g3_UpdateFullTransform()` then composites: `gTransformFull = ViewPort × Projection × ModelView`.
  - **Verification:** With z-bias=0: green quad should be hidden behind red (same depth, rendered second, but GL_LEQUAL should allow it through... actually with same Z, second render overwrites). With z-bias=5: green should definitely appear on top. Test with negative bias (-5.0): green should be hidden behind red.

- [ ] **Polygon offset: coplanar rendering**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `PolygonOffsetCoplanar`
  - **Setup:** Two quads at same position. Red first, green second.
  - **Render:**
    1. `rend_SetCoplanarPolygonOffset(0.0f)` — disabled. Render red.
    2. `rend_SetCoplanarPolygonOffset(1.0f)` — enabled. Render green.
  - **Internal logic:** When factor != 0: `glEnable(GL_POLYGON_OFFSET_FILL)` with `glPolygonOffset(-1.0f, -1.0f)`. Negative offset pushes fragments closer to camera.
  - **Verification:** With offset enabled, green quad should appear on top of red (no z-fighting). With offset=0, there may be z-fighting artifacts (alternating red/green pixels) depending on exact depth values.

- [ ] **Z-buffer clear and re-use**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ZBufferClearReuse`
  - **Setup:** Render frame 1: red quad at z=-200. End frame.
  - **Render frame 2:** Start new frame, `rend_ClearZBuffer()`, render blue quad at z=-300 (behind where red was).
  - **Verification:** Blue quad should render correctly — z-buffer was cleared, old depth values from red quad don't interfere. If z-buffer wasn't properly cleared, blue might be occluded by stale depth data.

### Clipping

- [ ] **Frustum clipping: polygon off-screen left**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ClipPolygonLeft`
  - **Setup:** Create a quad positioned mostly off the left edge of the screen. Camera at (0,0,-500) looking +Z. Quad center at x=-400, z=-300.
  - **Render:** `g3_RotatePoint()` to get clip codes. `p3_codes` should have `CC_OFF_LEFT` set for some vertices. Call `g3_DrawPoly()`.
  - **Verification:** Only the portion of the quad that falls within the viewport should be rendered. The visible portion should be a truncated polygon (not the full quad). No rendering artifacts at the left edge.

- [ ] **Frustum clipping: polygon off-screen right/top/bottom**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ClipPolygonRight`, `ClipPolygonTop`, `ClipPolygonBottom`
  - **Same pattern as left clipping** but position quad at x=+400 (right), y=+400 (top), y=-400 (bottom).
  - **Verification:** Each test should show a properly clipped polygon at the respective edge.

- [ ] **Frustum clipping: polygon straddling two edges (corner)**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ClipPolygonCorner`
  - **Setup:** Quad positioned at top-right corner of screen (x=+400, y=+400, z=-300).
  - **Verification:** Should clip against both right and top planes. Output should be a small visible triangle or quad at the corner. `g3_ClipPolygon()` uses double-buffered vertex buffers and Sutherland-Hodgman algorithm, processing each plane sequentially.

### Clipper High-Risk Edge Cases

Research-identified highest-risk areas for the Sutherland-Hodgman clipper (confirmed from `HardwareClipper.cpp`, 385 lines):

- [ ] **Temp point pool exhaustion** — `ClipPolygonLeft` through `ClipPolygonCorner` tests should also verify behavior when polygons exceed pool capacity. Confirmed: `static g3Point temp_points[MAX_POINTS_IN_POLY]` with `MAX_POINTS_IN_POLY = 100` (from `HardwareInternal.h`). Feed a polygon with >50 vertices through all 6 frustum planes plus custom clip (worst case). Verify graceful handling or assert when the pool is exceeded. Pool uses stack-based LIFO: `static int free_point_num` pointer, `InitFreePoints()`, `FreeTempPoint()`.

- [ ] **Degenerate polygons after clipping** — A quad clipped by a plane parallel to one edge can collapse to a line or point. Test: create a quad where 3 vertices are off-screen on the same side. After clipping, verify output has ≥3 vertices (valid polygon) or is correctly discarded.

- [ ] **Precision near clip planes** — Place vertices at `w * 0.999` and `w * 1.001` relative to the near plane (Sutherland-Hodgman's most numerically unstable case). Test with camera very close to polygon edges. Verify no rendering artifacts, no missing vertices. The `ClipEdge()` formula `k = (a - z) / ((a - z) - b + z_off)` is where precision loss occurs.

- [ ] **UV/color interpolation across clip edges** — The intersection point must interpolate UVs and vertex colors linearly in clip space. Confirmed: `ClipPlane()` interpolates UV, lighting, and RGBA along clipped edges. Test: textured gradient polygon clipped at 45 degrees. Verify no seams or color banding at the clip edge by sampling pixels along the boundary.

- [ ] **Double-buffered array swap correctness** — Confirmed: `static g3Point *Vbuf0[MAX_POINTS_IN_POLY]` (ping) and `static g3Point *Vbuf1[MAX_POINTS_IN_POLY]` (pong). After clipping against each plane, the output buffer pointer swaps and vertex count updates. Test by clipping a polygon against all 6 planes sequentially and verifying the final vertex list is in the correct buffer. Each plane uses bit flags: left=1, right=2, top=4, bottom=8, far=16, custom=32.

- [ ] **Far clip plane**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FarClipPlane`
  - **Setup:** 3 quads at z=-200, z=-600, z=-1000. Default far clip is FLT_MAX.
  - **Test A:** `g3_SetFarClipZ(500.0f)`. Only the z=-200 quad should be visible.
  - **Test B:** `g3_SetFarClipZ(800.0f)`. z=-200 and z=-600 should be visible.
  - **Internal logic (confirmed):** `CC_OFF_FAR` set when `p3_z > Far_clip_z` (extern `float Far_clip_z` in `HardwareInternal.h`). Clipped by `ClipFarEdge()` with formula: `k = 1.0f - ((z_off - Far_clip_z) / (z_off - z_on))`.
  - **Verification:** Test A: count visible quads. Expected: 1. Test B: Expected: 2.

- [ ] **Custom clip plane**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `CustomClipPlane`
  - **Setup:** Full-screen quad at z=-300. Set a custom clip plane that cuts the quad in half diagonally.
  - **Render:**
    1. Define plane: point at origin, normal pointing up-right: `normal = (1, 1, 0)` normalized
    2. `g3_SetCustomClipPlane(1, &plane_point, &plane_normal)`
    3. Render the quad
  - **Internal logic (confirmed):** Extern vars in `HardwareInternal.h`: `uint8_t Clip_custom`, `vector Clip_plane`, `float Clip_plane_distance`. `CC_OFF_CUSTOM` set when `dot(plane_normal, point - plane_point) < -0.005`. Clipped by `ClipCustomEdge()` using ray-plane intersection: `k = vm_Dot3Product(Clip_plane, w) / vm_Dot3Product(Clip_plane, ray_direction)`.
  - **Verification:** Only the portion of the quad on the "positive" side of the plane should render. Should see a diagonal cut across the quad.

- [ ] **Line clipping**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ClipLine`
  - **Setup:** Draw a line from off-screen left (-500, 240) to off-screen right (1100, 240) — horizontal line crossing the full viewport width.
  - **Render:** Use `rend_TransformSetToPassthru()` for 2D screen coordinates, then `rend_DrawLine()`. Or use `g3_DrawLine()` with 3D points that project off-screen.
  - **Internal logic (confirmed):** `ClipLine()` in `HardwareInternal.h` clips a line segment against the viewing pyramid. The clipper iterates through each plane flag (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32), ensures the "on-screen" point is p0, then clips p1 against that plane via `ClipEdge()`. Updates OR codes and frees old temp p1 if temporary.
  - **Verification:** Line should be visible only within the viewport bounds (x=0 to x=639, y=0 to y=479). Endpoints should be clipped to viewport edge. No rendering outside viewport.

- [ ] **Back-face culling: `g3_CheckNormalFacing`**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `BackfaceCulling`
  - **Setup:** A quad with a known normal pointing toward the camera (facing) and the same quad with normal pointing away (back-facing).
  - **Test A:** Normal = (0, 0, 1) (facing camera at z=-500). `g3_CheckNormalFacing(&center, &normal)` should return true.
  - **Test B:** Normal = (0, 0, -1) (away from camera). `g3_CheckNormalFacing()` should return false.
  - **Internal logic:** Computes dot product of (view_position - point) with normal. Positive dot product = facing.
  - **Verification:** Test A: returns true. Test B: returns false.

- [ ] **`g3_CheckAndDrawPoly`: cull + draw**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `CheckAndDrawPoly`
  - **Setup:** Two quads: one facing camera, one back-facing.
  - **Render:** Call `g3_CheckAndDrawPoly(4, pointlist, 0, &normal, &center)` for each.
  - **Verification:** Only the facing quad should be rendered. The back-facing quad should be skipped. Count rendered polygons: expected 1.

### Instancing

- [ ] **`g3_StartInstanceMatrix`: render object with transform**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `InstanceMatrix`
  - **Setup:** Render a base quad at origin (red). Then render the same quad 4 times using instance matrices: translated to positions (±200, ±200, -300), each rotated 45° around Z axis.
  - **Render:**
    1. Render base quad at origin
    2. For each instance position:
       a. Create orientation matrix (rotation 45° around Z)
       b. `g3_StartInstanceMatrix(&position, &orientation)`
       c. Render the same quad (same vertex data, but in local coords at origin)
       d. `g3_DoneInstance()`
  - **Internal logic:** `g3_StartInstanceMatrix` saves View_matrix, View_position, Unscaled_matrix, and gTransformModelView to stack. Transforms: `View_position = (View_position - pos) * orient`, `View_matrix = (~orient) * View_matrix`.
  - **Verification:** Should see 5 quads total: 1 at origin, 4 rotated 45° at the offset positions. All quads should have the same size and shape (just transformed).

- [ ] **`g3_StartInstanceAngles`: render with Euler angles**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `InstanceAngles`
  - **Setup:** Same as above but use `angvec` (pitch, bank, heading) instead of matrix.
  - **Render:**
    1. `angvec angles; angles.p = 0; angles.b = 0; angles.h = PI/4;`
    2. `g3_StartInstanceAngles(&position, &angles)`
  - **Verification:** Same visual result as matrix version — 4 rotated quads at offset positions.

- [ ] **Nested instances (depth 2+)**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `InstanceNested`
  - **Setup:** Render a "parent" quad. Instance a "child" quad relative to parent. Instance a "grandchild" quad relative to child.
  - **Render:**
    1. Render parent at origin
    2. `g3_StartInstanceMatrix(&parent_pos, &parent_orient)`
    3. Render child at local origin (which is parent's position)
    4. `g3_StartInstanceMatrix(&child_local_pos, &child_orient)`
    5. Render grandchild at local origin (which is child's position in parent's frame)
    6. `g3_DoneInstance()` (exit grandchild)
    7. `g3_DoneInstance()` (exit child)
  - **Internal logic:** Instance stack depth is 30. Each push saves the current view state. Nested transforms compose correctly.
  - **Verification:** Grandchild should appear at the composed transform (parent_pos + parent_orient * child_local_pos + parent_orient * child_orient * grandchild_local_pos). Verify position matches expected composed transform.

- [ ] **Instance stack push/pop correctness**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `InstanceStackRestore`
  - **Setup:** Render a quad at origin (reference). Push instance, render, pop. Render another quad at origin — should appear in the same place as the first.
  - **Verification:** After `g3_DoneInstance()`, View_position and View_matrix should be restored to pre-push state. The post-pop quad should perfectly overlap the pre-push quad. If stack restore is broken, the post-pop quad would appear offset.

- [ ] **Instancing: mathematical transform verification**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `InstanceMathVerification`
  - **Rationale:** Mathematical verification is superior to visual comparison for instancing. Visual tests miss precision drift that math catches — critical given VC6 float math compatibility issues.
  - **Confirmed pipeline:** `g3_UpdateFullTransform()` composes `gTransformFull = gTransformViewPort × gTransformProjection × gTransformModelView`. `g3_GetModelViewMatrix()` builds MV from rvec/uvec/fvec with Z_bias at `mvMat[14]`. `g3_TransformMult()` does standard 4x4 multiplication.
  - **Test plan:**
    1. Inject a known modelview-projection matrix stack
    2. Transform canonical points (unit cube vertices) through the stack
    3. Verify resulting clip-space and screen-space positions match expected matrix multiplication chain
    4. Test push/pop to maximum depth (30) and verify final transform equals identity or expected composed transform
    5. Test angle nesting: apply rotation instances, verify `atan2` of resulting vectors matches expected angles
  - **Verification:** Compute expected positions via CPU-side matrix multiplication using same `g3_TransformMult()` formula: `temp[y][x] = Σ(a[y][i] * b[i][x])`. Compare with renderer output (read back transformed vertex positions or verify screen-space projection matches expected values within float epsilon tolerance). `g3_ForceTransformRefresh()` resets `sUseTransformPassthru = -1` to force recalculation.

  - **Render:** `g3_DrawBox(GR_RGB(255, 255, 255), &point, 50.0f)`
  - **Internal logic:** Projects point, computes half-width `w = rad * Matrix_scale.x() * Window_w2 / p3_z`, draws 4 wireframe lines forming a box.
  - **Verification:** Should see a white wireframe square (projected box) centered at the point's screen position. Size depends on depth: closer = larger, farther = smaller.

- [ ] **Rotated bitmap (`g3_DrawRotatedBitmap`)**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `RotatedBitmap`
  - **Setup:** Create a 32x32 bitmap with a visible pattern (e.g., "L" shape or arrow pointing up).
  - **Render:**
    1. `g3_DrawRotatedBitmap(&pos, 0, 32, 32, bm_handle)` — no rotation (should point up)
    2. `g3_DrawRotatedBitmap(&pos2, PI/2, 32, 32, bm_handle)` — 90° rotation (should point right)
  - **Internal logic:** Creates rotation matrix from `rot_angle`, computes 4 corners by rotating offset vectors by the rotation matrix, then transforms by view orientation.
  - **Verification:** Bitmap 1: pattern oriented normally (up). Bitmap 2: pattern rotated 90° (right). Check corner positions and pixel patterns to confirm rotation angle.

- [ ] **Planar rotated bitmap**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `PlanarRotatedBitmap`

  - **Render:** `g3_DrawPlanarRotatedBitmap(&pos, &normal, rot_angle, 32, 32, bm_handle)`
  - **Internal logic:** Creates matrix from normal (`vm_VectorToMatrix`), transposes it, creates twist matrix from rotation angle, computes 4 corners in twist frame, transforms by normal matrix.
  - **Verification:** Bitmap should appear rotated within the plane defined by the normal. Compare with `g3_DrawRotatedBitmap` — planar version respects the plane orientation.

  - **Internal logic:** Gets view orientation matrix, computes 4 corners using `uvec` and `rvec` scaled by width/height, rotates each point, sets UVs 0..1.
  - **Verification:** Bitmap should appear facing the camera (always oriented to view), centered at position, 100x100 pixels. Unlike rotated bitmap, this one automatically faces the viewer.

## Phase 5: Advanced / Special Features

- [ ] **Gamma correction**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `GammaCorrection`
  - **Setup:** Render a gray quad (128, 128, 128).
  - **Test A:** `rend_SetGammaValue(1.0f)` — no correction.
  - **Test B:** `rend_SetGammaValue(2.2f)` — standard gamma.
  - **Shader logic:** `out_color.rgb = pow(out_color.rgb, vec3(1.0 / u_gamma))`. At gamma=2.2, 128/255 ≈ 0.5 → 0.5^(1/2.2) ≈ 0.73 → pixel ≈ (186, 186, 186).
  - **Verification:** Test B pixels should be brighter than Test A pixels. Check pixel value matches expected gamma-corrected output (±10 tolerance).

- [ ] **Gamma: SRGB framebuffer vs shader gamma**
  - **Rationale:** Moving from fixed-function to programmable pipeline often breaks gamma because `GL_FRAMEBUFFER_SRGB` behavior differs from manual `pow()` in shader.
  - **Test:** Verify that the shader gamma path produces identical output to what an SRGB framebuffer would produce. If the engine uses `GL_FRAMEBUFFER_SRGB`, test both paths.

- [ ] **Texture format: 15-bit color (RGB5_A1)**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `TextureFormat15Bit`
  - **Rationale:** 15-bit textures break frequently in legacy ports due to alignment/packing differences.
  - **Test:** Create a bitmap in 1551 format with known pixel values. Render textured quad. Verify output colors match expected 15-bit→display conversion.

- [ ] **Texture format: non-power-of-two with GL_REPEAT**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `NPOTRepeat`
  - **Rationale:** NPOT textures with `GL_REPEAT` are not universally supported and may fall back to `GL_CLAMP_TO_EDGE` silently.
  - **Test:** Create 48x48 bitmap (NPOT). Set `WT_WRAP`. Render quad with UV (0,0) to (4,4). Verify pattern repeats (not clamped).

- [ ] **Scissor test**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `ScissorClipRegion`
  - **Setup:** Render a full-screen quad. Enable scissor test to clip to a smaller region.
  - **Render:** Check how scissor is set — likely via `rend_TransformSetViewport()` or direct GL call.
  - **Verification:** Only the scissored region should show the quad. Outside region should be background color.

- [ ] **Framebuffer copy**

  - **Render:**
    1. Render a scene
    2. `rend_SetFrameBufferCopyState(true)`
    3. `rend_CopyBitmapToFramebuffer(bm_handle, x, y)`
  - **Verification:** Bitmap content should be copied into the framebuffer at position (x, y). Verify pixel content at that position matches the bitmap data.

- [ ] **Aspect ratio override**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `SetAspectRatio`
  - **Setup:** Render a circle (should appear circular).
  - **Test A:** `g3_SetAspectRatio(1.0f)` — square pixels.
  - **Test B:** `g3_SetAspectRatio(1.78f)` — widescreen.
  - **Verification:** Test A: circle appears round. Test B: circle appears stretched horizontally (or projection is adjusted to maintain correct proportions in widescreen).

- [ ] **Viewport manipulation**

  - **Render:** `rend_TransformSetViewport(lx, ty, width, height)` to set a sub-region viewport.
  - **Verification:** All subsequent rendering should be clipped to the specified viewport region. Content outside the viewport should not be visible.

- [ ] **Projection/modelview matrix injection**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `InjectProjectionMatrix`
  - **Render:** Construct a custom projection matrix (e.g., oblique projection). Call `rend_TransformSetProjection(matrix)`.
  - **Verification:** Rendered scene should use the custom projection. Parallel lines should converge differently than with perspective projection.

- [ ] **Delta vector rotation**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `DeltaVectorRotation`
  - **Render:**
    1. Create a vector `v = (1, 0, 0)`
    2. `g3_RotateDeltaX(&result, 0.5f)` — rotate around X by 0.5 radians
    3. `g3_RotateDeltaY(&result2, 0.5f)` — rotate around Y
    4. `g3_RotateDeltaZ(&result3, 0.5f)` — rotate around Z
  - **Verification:** Result vectors should match expected rotation matrix multiplication. For X-rotation of (1,0,0): no change (X-axis rotation doesn't affect X component). For Y-rotation: x and z components change.

  - **Verification:** Result vector should point from camera through the screen position. Unproject and verify the direction matches expected world-space direction.

- [ ] **Point depth calculation**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `CalcPointDepth`
  - **Setup:** Create points at known Z depths: z=-100, z=-500, z=-1000.
  - **Render:** `g3_CalcPointDepth(&vec)` for each.
  - **Verification:** Depth values should increase with distance. z=-1000 should have larger depth value than z=-100.

  - **Verification:** Should see the polygon rendered with visible triangulation lines (diagonals showing how the polygon is split into triangles). With triangulation test off, only the filled polygon should be visible.

## Phase 6: Infrastructure Improvements

### Visual Regression: Hybrid SSIM + Pixel-Diff

**Problem:** Raw MD5 hashing of entire framebuffers is brittle — any single pixel difference (driver version changes, Mesa updates, floating-point variations) breaks the test.

**Recommended approach (hybrid):**

| Technique | Use Case | Tool |
|-----------|----------|------|
| **SSIM (Structural Similarity)** | Full-frame tests — tolerates minor noise while catching perceptual differences | `compare -metric SSIM` (ImageMagick) or `sharp` Node module |
| **Pixel-diff with tolerance** | Tests that should produce identical output (clear color, raw GL quads) | Custom comparator with ±N per-channel threshold |
| **Region-of-interest masking** | HUD/text tests where only a sub-rectangle matters | Define ROI per-test in metadata |
| **Diff mask generation** | On-failure highlighting in HTML report | Generate `{name}.diff.png` showing changed regions |

**Implementation:**
- Use SSIM threshold of ~0.98 for visual tests (fuzzier: fog, alpha blends, mipmaps)
- Use exact pixel-diff with ±2 tolerance for deterministic tests (clears, colored quads, lines)
- For the TypeScript runner, shell out to ImageMagick or use `sharp` to compute SSIM in-process
- Generate diff mask images on failure — overlay red highlights on changed pixels in the HTML report. The report tools are implemented in TypeScript/React: [`tests/report-tools-ts/README.md`](tests/report-tools-ts/README.md) (tree/DAG builders, React SSR, Tailwind v4, Vite SSG). Existing React components can be extended to show diff overlays.
- Store baseline images in `tests/render_output_baseline/` committed to version control

### Shader Validation

- [ ] **glslang CI check** — run Khronos reference compiler on `shaders/vertex.glsl` and `shaders/fragment.glsl` to validate syntax without requiring a GL context. Fast, catches compilation errors early.
- [ ] **piglit-style shader_runner tests** — define expected output for shader programs (fog computation, gamma correction, multitexture blend). Can run against llvmpipe to catch GLSL behavior differences across drivers.
- [ ] **dEQP subset** — run relevant drawElements Quality Program tests against llvmpipe for shader precision validation, especially for fog distance computation and gamma `pow()` correctness.
- [ ] **RenderDoc capture for debugging** — not for CI, but invaluable for test failures. Capture a failing test frame and inspect uniform values, texture bindings, and draw call state.

### Vertex Buffer and Shader Infrastructure Tests

Based on confirmed `ShaderProgram.h` implementation (260 lines):

- [ ] **OrphaningVertexBuffer: buffer wrap-around**
  - **Rationale:** `OrphaningVertexBuffer` has capacity of 65536 vertices (`1 << 16`). When full, it calls `glBufferData(..., nullptr, GL_STREAM_DRAW)` to orphan and re-allocate.
  - **Test:** Render a frame with >65536 vertices (e.g., 16384 quads = 65536 vertices exactly, then 16385 = overflow). Verify no crash, no missing geometry. The second buffer allocation should work seamlessly.
  - **Verification:** Count rendered polygons. Expected: all 16385 quads render. No vertex data corruption at the wrap boundary.

- [ ] **Uniform caching: rapid uniform changes**
  - **Rationale:** `ShaderProgram` caches uniform locations via `getUniformId()` to avoid repeated `glGetUniformLocation` lookups.
  - **Test:** Rapidly change uniforms between draw calls (e.g., fog color, alpha value) within the same frame. Verify the cache correctly returns the same uniform ID each time.
  - **Verification:** Render output should match expected values after each uniform change. No stale uniform values from cache.

- [ ] **RAII resource cleanup**
  - **Rationale:** All GPU resources use `MoveOnlyHolder` with custom deleters (`dglDeleteBuffers`, `dglDeleteVertexArrays`, `dglDeleteShader`, `dglDeleteProgram`).
  - **Test:** Create and destroy multiple `ShaderProgram` instances in sequence. Verify no GL errors, no leaked resources.
  - **Verification:** Run with `MESA_DEBUG=1` to detect resource leaks. Each program creation/destruction cycle should leave GL state clean.

### CI Integration

**Upstream CI alignment:** The Descent 3 repo already enables testing in GitHub Actions via `-DBUILD_TESTING=ON` and `ctest` for unit tests ([Actions workflow](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/actions/runs/9333236866/workflow)). Render tests added to this path would integrate with the existing build model rather than requiring a separate harness. Register render test executables with CTest alongside unit tests. Build instructions: [`BUILD.md`](BUILD.md).

- [ ] **Per-CI render tests on llvmpipe** — current approach, good for functional testing and regression detection.
  - Environment: `GALLIUM_DRIVER=llvmpipe`, `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1`, `MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE=3.3`
  - Add `MESA_DEBUG=flush` to catch synchronization issues that hardware drivers might hide
  - Add `MESA_NO_ERROR=1` during benchmark runs to reduce CPU overhead from error checking
- [ ] **Periodic real-hardware runs** — llvmpipe does not replicate hardware-specific precision quirks, texture sampling edge cases, or driver optimizations. Supplement with:
  - GitHub Actions GPU runners (NVIDIA available)
  - Self-hosted runners with NVIDIA/AMD cards
  - Weekly or per-PR for critical paths only
- [ ] **Baseline management:**
  - `--update-baseline` on main branch merges (or nightly)
  - PR branches: fail on SSIM < 0.98 or pixel-diff exceeds tolerance
  - Store per-branch baselines temporarily for comparison
- [ ] **State isolation enforcement** — `D3RenderTestBase::SetUp()` must aggressively reset ALL GL state: blend, depth, scissor, viewport, bound buffers, active texture unit, shader program. Prevents test ordering dependencies (a common legacy engine porting bug).

### Per-test Trace Verification

- [ ] Parse tracer JSON (`tests/tracer/tracer.cpp` + `-finstrument-functions`) and verify function call sequences match expected patterns:
  - Texturing test: `rend_SetTextureType` → `opengl_MakeTextureObject` → `opengl_TranslateBitmapToOpenGL` → `rend_DrawPolygon3D` → `glDrawArrays`
  - Fog test: `rend_SetFogState` → `rend_SetFogBorders` → `rend_SetFogColor` → draw → fragment shader fog path
  - Instancing test: `g3_StartInstanceMatrix` → ...draw... → `g3_DoneInstance`
- [ ] Flag unexpected function calls (e.g., `glEnable(GL_LIGHTING)` in a test that should use programmable pipeline)

### Test Data Generators

- [ ] **Helper functions in `tests/render/test_utils.h`:**
  - `GenerateCheckerboardBitmap(width, height, color1, color2)` — checkerboard pattern
  - `GenerateGradientBitmap(width, height, color_start, color_end, axis)` — linear gradient
  - `GenerateRadialBitmap(width, height, center_color, edge_color)` — radial gradient
  - `GenerateAlphaBitmap(width, height, pattern)` — alpha channel patterns (circular, linear, random)
  - `GenerateCircularAlphaBitmap(size, center_alpha, edge_alpha)` — for AT_TEXTURE tests
- [ ] **GoogleTest parameterized tests** — run same test logic across all alpha types: `::testing::Values(AT_CONSTANT, AT_TEXTURE, AT_VERTEX, AT_CONSTANT_TEXTURE, ...)`

### Performance Benchmarks

**Important:** In llvmpipe, absolute performance metrics (FPS, poly throughput) are NOT representative of real GPU performance — llvmpipe is CPU-bound and lacks GPU parallelism. Use llvmpipe for relative regression detection, real hardware for absolute numbers.

| Metric | How to Measure | Value in llvmpipe |
|--------|---------------|-------------------|
| Draw call count | Instrumentation counter in `D3RenderTestBase` | Catches batching regressions |
| Shader uniform binding time | CPU timestamp around `glUniform*` calls | Uniform update overhead |
| Texture upload bandwidth | Time `glTexImage2D`/`glTexSubImage2D` for known bitmaps | Catches cache eviction issues |
| Vertex buffer orphaning rate | Count `glBufferData` calls per frame | Streaming efficiency |
| State change count | Track `glEnable`/`glDisable`/`glUseProgram` | API overhead |

- [ ] Add `--benchmark` mode to runner: N iterations per test, report avg/min/max/stddev
- [ ] Output `benchmark_results.json` with per-test timing data
- [ ] Run benchmarks on real hardware weekly, llvmpipe per-CI for regression-only

### Render Test Documentation

- [ ] Per-test screenshot in `tests/render/docs/` with description of what the test verifies
- [ ] Per-test LaTeX PDF generated from `plans/3d_scene_latex_whitepaper.md` spec — each PDF explains CPU→GPU→raster causal chain (scene data → mathematical construction → API submission → GPU execution → framebuffer output)
- [ ] Known limitations documented per-suite (e.g., "CircleRender: Mesa stubs, requires HardwareOpenGL")
- [ ] Test coverage matrix auto-generated from test source files (parse `TEST_F` macros)

## Phase 7: Not Yet Implemented / Stubbed

These are features mentioned in headers or comments but not fully implemented. Worth investigating before testing.

- [ ] **Bumpmapping** — `rend_SupportsBumpmapping()` returns 0, `rend_SetBumpmapReadyState()` is stub. Investigate if bumpmapping was planned but never completed, or if it was removed. Check `renderer.h` comments and legacy code.

- [ ] **S3 texture compression** — mentioned in header comments (`GL_EXT_texture_compression_s3tc`), not visible in current texture upload code. Check if `dyna_gl.h` loads compressed texture extensions.

- [ ] **Mirrors** — mentioned in `renderer.h` comments (mirror rendering), no dedicated renderer code visible in current modules. Check `HardwareDraw.cpp` and `HardwareOpenGL.cpp` for mirror-related stubs.

- [ ] **Sky/cloud rendering** — `WT_WRAP_V` wrap type exists (clamped S, repeated T) specifically for sky textures, but no sky-specific renderer code. Sky rendering may be handled at the game/level layer, not the renderer.

- [ ] **Volumetric effects** — not implemented in renderer. May be handled by particle system or separate effects layer.

- [ ] **Corona/lens flare** — not implemented. Check if these are in game code (osiris scripts) rather than renderer.

- [ ] **LFB lock** — `rend_GetLFBLock()`, `rend_ReleaseLFBLock()` are stubs. LFB (Linear Frame Buffer) access was for software renderer direct memory access, which was removed.

- [ ] **Software renderer parameters** — `rend_SetSoftwareParameters()` is stub. Software renderer code was removed in PRs #305, #321. This function may be removable entirely.

## Phase 8: Complex Geometry & Batch

- [ ] **Teapot or multi-polygon batch**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `BatchRender`
  - **Setup:** Render many quads/cubes in one frame using shared helpers.
  - **Render:** Multiple `rend_DrawPolygon3D` per frame, single `g3_StartFrame`/`g3_EndFrame`.
  - **Verification:** Verify no crash, state preserved, depth ordering correct.

- [ ] **Degenerate geometry: zero-area poly**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `DegeneratePolygon`
  - **Setup:** Create polygon with collinear vertices or zero area.
  - **Verification:** Should skip or render without crash. No validation errors.

- [ ] **Vertex/polygon limits**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `VertexLimitTest`
  - **Setup:** Create polygon at or near max vertices per poly (`MAX_POINTS_IN_POLY = 100`).
  - **Verification:** Assert no crash, graceful cull if exceeding limits.

- [ ] **Large coordinates**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `LargeCoordinateTest`
  - **Setup:** Place geometry at very large world coordinates (e.g., 100000+ units from origin).
  - **Verification:** Verify no precision loss, no rendering artifacts.

- [ ] **Frame state: sequential frames**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `FrameIsolation`
  - **Setup:** Two sequential frames with different state (e.g., red vs blue).
  - **Verification:** Verify no cross-contamination between frames.

- [ ] **Mixed primitives in one frame**
  - **File:** `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` — new test `MixedPrimitives`
  - **Setup:** Render lines + quads + points in single frame.
  - **Verification:** All primitives render correctly, no state conflicts.

## Phase 9: HUD / Text Rendering

### 9.1 Font Pipeline & Basic Text
- [ ] **Font load success** — Load `hihud.fnt`, verify handle is valid, no leaks on unload.
- [ ] **Single character** — Render 'A', verify glyph appears at expected position.
- [ ] **Short string** — Render "Hello World", verify full string visible.
- [ ] **Long string** — Render string exceeding viewport width, verify clipping/wrapping.
- [ ] **Special chars** — Digits, punctuation, extended ASCII.
- [ ] **Formatted output** — `grtext_Printf` with format specifiers.

### 9.2 Color & Alpha
- [ ] **HUD green** — Default HUD color rendering.
- [ ] **Color variations** — All HUD color constants.
- [ ] **Alpha blending** — Semi-transparent text over backgrounds.

### 9.3 Layout & Positioning
- [ ] **Coordinate system** — Origin (top-left vs center), viewport sizes (640x480, 1920x1080).
- [ ] **Multi-line text** — Line breaks, proper spacing.
- [ ] **Clipping/overflow** — Text at viewport edges, partial visibility.

### 9.4 HUD Elements & Widgets
- [ ] **HUD layout structure** — Info display (score, health placeholders).
- [ ] **Menu/dialog stubs** — Where applicable without full game state.

### 9.5 Advanced Text & Performance
- [ ] **String measurement** — Width/height metrics if APIs exposed.
- [ ] **Rotation/scaling** — If supported by font system.
- [ ] **Batching/memory checks** — Frame-time impact.

### 9.6 Edge Cases & Integration
- [ ] **Invalid input** — Null string, empty, very long.
- [ ] **Missing font asset** — Graceful fallback behavior.
- [ ] **HUD state sync** — Common layouts (minimal HUD, full HUD).

### 9.7 Localization & Text Variants
- [ ] **Multi-language** — If supported, test character sets.
- [ ] **Bold/Italic/Underline** — Styled text if available.
- [ ] **Combined styles** — Bold + Color, Italic + Underline.

---

## Phase 10: Reference Output & Validation

### 10.1 Visual Regression Testing
**Objective:** Detect unintended visual changes

**Approach:**
- **Golden Image Baseline** — Render standard HUD configurations, capture screenshots, store as reference images, commit to repository
- **Continuous Comparison** — Render current version, compare pixel-by-pixel, generate diff images, flag significant changes
- **Tolerance Levels** — Pixel-perfect match (strict), histogram similarity > 99% (loose), perceptual difference < threshold
- **CI/CD Integration** — Run on every commit, fail build if regression > threshold, generate visual report, track regression trend over time

**Implementation:**
- Use image comparison library (OpenCV, ImageMagick, etc.)
- Store baseline images in test data directory
- Generate diff overlays for review

### 10.2 Text Readability Metrics
**Objective:** Validate HUD text remains readable

**Metrics:**
- **Contrast Ratio (WCAG standard)** — Text color vs background, minimum 4.5:1 (normal), minimum 3:1 (large text)
- **Font Size** — Minimum readable size, typical size (14-16pt), large size (18pt+)
- **Line Length** — Max characters per line, proper line breaking
- **Accessibility** — Color-blind friendly, high contrast mode support, HUD customization options

---

## BOA / Visibility System Testing

The BOA (Big Ol' Array) Potentially Visible Set system is a documented fragile area ([wiki](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/wiki/Descent-3-Internals), [Issue #105](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/issues/105), [Issue #279](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/issues/279)). Weekly updates confirm a fix was applied ("BOA Not Valid silenced, max render distance bumped").

While BOA lives at the game/level layer rather than the renderer, rendering errors from BOA failures are visible and testable:

- [ ] **BOA checksum validation** — Load a known level (e.g., SECRET2.DEM) and verify the BOA checksum matches expected value. If checksum fails, the engine recomputes geometry — verify this produces correct rendering output.
- [ ] **Room visibility: simple case** — Create a minimal two-room level with a single portal between them. Position camera in Room A, verify Room B's geometry renders (visible through portal). Position camera so portal is behind, verify Room B is culled.
- [ ] **Room visibility: portal chain** — Three rooms connected A→B→C. Camera in A should see B (direct portal) and C (through B). Verify render output shows both rooms' geometry.
- [ ] **Room visibility: stacked rooms** — Complex portal topology where multiple rooms are visible through different portals. Verify all visible rooms render, no rooms incorrectly culled.
- [ ] **Max render distance** — The weekly update mentions max render distance was bumped. Test: place geometry at various distances from camera. Verify geometry within max distance renders, beyond max distance is culled. Compare with pre-fix behavior if baselines exist.
- [ ] **Demo playback rendering** — Record a short demo (or use SECRET2.DEM), play it back, and verify rendered frames match expected output. BOA errors manifest as missing geometry or incorrect room visibility during playback.

### Known Community Issues and Fragile Areas

- **BOA (Big Ol' Array) "Not Valid!" errors** — The Potentially Visible Set system triggers rendering errors when level checksums fail due to VC6 float math differences vs modern compilers. Affects level loading and demo playback. Fix confirmed in [Weekly Updates](https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3/wiki/Weekly-Updates).
- **Timer precision** — High framerates are not displayed accurately on the HUD due to timer precision limitations. Not rendering-critical but affects benchmark output.
- **Platform-specific bugs** — The `icculus/descent3-bugs` repository tracks Linux/Mac rendering issues. Cross-reference before marking tests as passing.
- **VC6 float math compatibility** — Any test involving matrix transforms, projection, or depth calculations should account for precision differences between legacy VC6 and modern compilers. The BOA checksum issue is a direct example of this problem.

### Missing Categories and Common Porting Bugs

- [ ] **Gamma correction consistency** — Moving from fixed-function to programmable pipeline often breaks gamma handling because `GL_FRAMEBUFFER_SRGB` behavior differs from manual `pow()` in shader. Test both SRGB framebuffer output AND the shader gamma path (`u_gamma` uniform).
- [ ] **Texture format edge cases** — 15-bit color textures (`RGB5_A1`), paletted textures, non-power-of-two textures with `GL_REPEAT` vs `GL_CLAMP_TO_EDGE`. These break frequently in legacy ports.
- [ ] **Z-fighting from matrix precision** — The programmable pipeline may compute MVP matrices with different precision than fixed-function. The existing Z-bias and polygon offset tests cover this, but also test coplanar polygons with identical vertex positions.
- [ ] **Fog coordinate vs shader fog** — Legacy OpenGL used `glFogCoordf` or per-vertex fog; D3's shader uses distance-based linear fog. Test that fog fades match expected behavior for large outdoor terrain scenes (distance from camera, not Z depth).
- [ ] **State leaking between tests** — `D3RenderTestBase` must aggressively reset all GL state in `SetUp()`. Add a "state leak detection" test that runs after every test and verifies default state.

---

## llvmpipe Limitations and CI Strategy

**Mesa llvmpipe** is the industry-standard software rasterizer for headless OpenGL CI. It uses LLVM JIT compilation of shader code, so GLSL shader logic executes correctly and catches most algorithmic bugs. However, it differs from NVIDIA/AMD hardware:

| Limitation | Impact on D3 Tests | Mitigation |
|-----------|-------------------|------------|
| No hardware precision quirks | Matrix/depth tests may pass on llvmpipe but fail on GPU | Periodic real-hardware CI runs |
| Texture sampling edge cases differ | Wrap mode, filtering, mip boundary tests may not catch GPU-specific bugs | Test with multiple Mesa versions |
| No driver optimizations | Shader recompilation, UBO handling absent — benchmarks meaningless | Use llvmpipe for regression only, not absolute perf |
| CPU-bound, no GPU parallelism | Poly throughput, draw call overhead not representative | Benchmark on real hardware weekly |

**Environment variables for CI:**
- `GALLIUM_DRIVER=llvmpipe` — force software rasterizer
- `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1` — disable hardware acceleration
- `MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE=3.3` — match our GL 3.2 Core + minor bump
- `MESA_DEBUG=flush` — catch synchronization issues hardware drivers hide
- `MESA_NO_ERROR=1` — reduce CPU overhead during benchmark runs (not for functional tests)

**Multi-backend strategy:** If the community ports to Vulkan or Metal, tests written against the abstract D3 API (`rend_*/g3_*`) will be far more valuable than raw GL command tests. The abstraction layer is the contract. Maintain a small suite of backend-specific integration tests (e.g., verify `rend_SetTexture` reaches correct sampler state via RenderDoc capture), but primary test coverage should be API-level.

---

## Implementation Task Ordering

### Track A: Game Suite (D3 stack only)

**Existing:** `d3_render_tests_game.cpp` has `D3GameRenderTest` with `RendererStateAndClear` and `RendererDrawLine`. Base uses `BeginFrame()`/`EndFrame()` but does not yet call `g3_StartFrame()`/`g3_EndFrame()` or `rend_DrawPolygon3D()`.

**Execution order:** A1 → A2 → A3 → A4 → A5 → A6 → A7 → A8 → A9 → A10. Each step builds on the previous; cube/quad and g3_StartFrame are required before perspective/depth/lighting.

| Step | Description | Phase Mapping |
|------|-------------|---------------|
| A1 | Add test helpers/fixtures, ensure g3_StartFrame/EndFrame in place | Foundation |
| A2 | Cube test: 8 vertices, 6 quad faces, projection sanity | Phase 2.1 |
| A3 | Quad test: 4 vertices, UV (0,0)–(1,1), vertex colors | Phase 2.2 |
| A4 | Perspective and clipping: camera at (0,0,-500), p3_codes | Phase 3 |
| A5 | Depth and visibility: two overlapping quads, Z-ordering | Phase 4 |
| A6 | Lighting and color: per-vertex, flat vs Gouraud | Phase 5 |
| A7 | Texture and UV: wrap modes, overlay/blend modes | Phase 6 |
| A8 | Complex geometry and batch: multi-polygon, state preserved | Phase 7/8 |
| A9 | Edge cases: degenerate poly, vertex limits, large coords | Phase 8/9 |
| A10 | Integration: frame state, mixed primitives, baseline comparison | Phase 10 |

### Track B: HUD Suite (text and HUD)

**Existing:** `d3_render_tests_hud.cpp` has `D3TextFixture` with font load, `RenderText`, color helpers, and tests like `RenderHudGreen`, `RenderHudColors`.

**Execution order:** B1 → B2 → B3 → B4 → B5 → B6 → B7. Foundation and basic text first, then layout and HUD elements, then advanced and edge cases.

| Step | Description | Phase Mapping |
|------|-------------|---------------|
| B1 | Font init, load success, metrics, no leaks | Phase 9.1 |
| B2 | Single char, short/long strings, special chars, formatted | Phase 9.1-9.2 |
| B3 | Coordinate system, viewport sizes, multi-line, clipping | Phase 9.3 |
| B4 | HUD layout, info display, menu/dialog stubs | Phase 9.4 |
| B5 | String measurement, rotation/scaling, batching/memory | Phase 9.5 |
| B6 | Invalid input, missing font, HUD state sync | Phase 9.6 |
| B7 | Multi-language, styled text, visual regression | Phase 9.7/10 |

Cross-track: Game and HUD can be advanced in parallel; only shared infra (e.g., `render_test_base.h`, EGL/context) is common.

### File and CMake

- **Game:** All new game tests and helpers in `tests/render/d3_render_tests_game.cpp` and (optional) `tests/render/fixtures/cube.h`, `tests/render/test_utils.h`. If new source files are added, register them in `tests/render/CMakeLists.txt` for the `game` suite.
- **HUD:** All new HUD tests in `tests/render/d3_render_tests_hud.cpp`. No new suites; existing `hud` executable stays.
- **Runner:** `tests/render/run_render_tests.ts` already lists `d3_render_tests_game` and `d3_render_tests_hud`; no change unless adding a new binary.

### Success Criteria

| Track | Phase | Metric |
| ----- | ----- | ------ |
| Game | A1 | Framework compiles; g3_StartFrame/EndFrame in place; existing tests pass |
| Game | A2–A3 | Cube and quad render without crash; basic assertions |
| Game | A4–A5 | Perspective and Z-ordering correct |
| Game | A6–A7 | Lighting and UV/texture as expected |
| Game | A8–A10 | Batch/complex + edge cases + frame isolation; optional baseline |
| HUD | B1–B2 | Font load and basic text at position/color |
| HUD | B3–B4 | Layout and HUD elements covered |
| HUD | B5–B7 | Advanced text, performance, edge cases, optional regression |

Implement phases in order; mark each task in progress then complete before moving to the next. Skip or stub polymodel/asset-heavy tests until asset paths and cfile/HOG setup are defined.
