### Test 60: AlphaTypeCoverage (Parameterized)

```cpp
class AlphaTypeTest : public D3GameRenderTest,
                       public testing::WithParamInterface<int8_t> {
};

TEST_P(AlphaTypeTest, OverlayWithAlphaType) {
  BeginFrame();
  rend_SetZBufferState(0);
  rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE);
  rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB);
  rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(0, 0, 0));

  rend_TransformSetToPassthru();
  glViewport(0, 0, width_, height_);
  glMatrixMode(GL_PROJECTION);
  glLoadIdentity();
  glOrtho(0, static_cast<GLdouble>(width_), static_cast<GLdouble>(height_), 0, 0, 1);
  glMatrixMode(GL_MODELVIEW);
  glLoadIdentity();

  // Background: gray rectangle
  rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(128, 128, 128));
  rend_FillRect(100, 100, 500, 400);

  // Foreground: semi-transparent based on alpha type
  rend_SetAlphaType(GetParam());
  rend_SetAlphaValue(128);
  rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(255, 0, 0));
  rend_FillRect(200, 150, 400, 350);

  EndFrame();
  ReadPixels();

  std::string name = "AlphaType_" + std::to_string(GetParam());
  SavePNG(name.c_str());

  // Sample the overlap region — verify it differs from both pure gray and pure red
  int overlap_r = GetFramebuffer()[(250 * width_ + 300) * 4];
  int overlap_g = GetFramebuffer()[(250 * width_ + 300) * 4 + 1];

  // With alpha blending, overlap should have both red and some green from background
  EXPECT_GT(overlap_r, 128) << "Overlap should have red component from foreground";
  (void)overlap_g;
}

INSTANTIATE_TEST_SUITE_P(
  AlphaTypes,
  AlphaTypeTest,
  testing::Values(AT_ALWAYS, AT_CONSTANT, AT_TEXTURE_MAP, AT_VERTEX)
);
```

### Test 61: ResolutionVariation

```cpp
// Test rendering at different resolutions using SetSoftwareParameters
TEST_F(D3GameRenderTest, ResolutionVariation) {
  struct {
    int w, h;
    const char *name;
  } configs[] = {
    {320, 240, "Res_320x240"},
    {640, 480, "Res_640x480"},
    {800, 600, "Res_800x600"},
  };

  for (auto &cfg : configs) {
    // Reconfigure the framebuffer
    rend_SetZBufferState(0);
    rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE);
    rend_SetAlphaType(AT_ALWAYS);
    rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB);
    rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(0, 0, 64));

    glViewport(0, 0, cfg.w, cfg.h);
    glMatrixMode(GL_PROJECTION);
    glLoadIdentity();
    glOrtho(0, static_cast<GLdouble>(cfg.w), static_cast<GLdouble>(cfg.h), 0, 0, 1);
    glMatrixMode(GL_MODELVIEW);
    glLoadIdentity();

    rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(255, 255, 255));
    rend_FillRect(cfg.w / 4, cfg.h / 4, cfg.w * 3 / 4, cfg.h * 3 / 4);

    ReadPixels();
    SavePNG(cfg.name);

    // Verify: center of the rect should be white
    int cx = cfg.w / 2, cy = cfg.h / 2;
    int idx = (cy * width_ + cx) * 4;
    uint8_t r = GetFramebuffer()[idx];
    EXPECT_GT(r, 200) << cfg.name << " center should be white";
  }
}
```

```cpp
TEST_F(D3GameRenderTest, ProjectionParametersQuery) {
  BeginFrame();
  rend_SetZBufferState(0);
  rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE);
  rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(0, 0, 0));

  int width = 0, height = 0;
  rend_GetProjectionParameters(&width, &height);

  int screenLX, screenTY, screenW, screenH;
  rend_GetProjectionScreenParameters(screenLX, screenTY, screenW, screenH);

  EndFrame();

  // Verify: parameters match test dimensions
  EXPECT_EQ(width, width_ - 1) << "Projection width should match test width";
  EXPECT_EQ(height, height_ - 1) << "Projection height should match test height";
  EXPECT_EQ(screenW, width_) << "Screen width should match test width";
  EXPECT_EQ(screenH, height_) << "Screen height should match test height";
}
```

### Test 66: MultiFrameAnimationSequence

```cpp
// Simulate an animation sequence across multiple frames
TEST_F(D3GameRenderTest, MultiFrameAnimationSequence) {
  const int num_frames = 16;
  int frame_count_with_pixels = 0;

  for (int f = 0; f < num_frames; f++) {
    BeginFrame();
    rend_SetZBufferState(0);
    rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE);
    rend_SetAlphaType(AT_ALWAYS);
    rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB);
    rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(0, 0, 0));

    rend_TransformSetToPassthru();
    glViewport(0, 0, width_, height_);
    glMatrixMode(GL_PROJECTION);
    glLoadIdentity();
    glOrtho(0, static_cast<GLdouble>(width_), static_cast<GLdouble>(height_), 0, 0, 1);
    glMatrixMode(GL_MODELVIEW);
    glLoadIdentity();

    // Animate: circle of quads rotating around center
    float angle = (f * 22.5f) * 3.14159265f / 180.0f;
    float radius = 100.0f;
    float cx = width_ / 2.0f + std::cos(angle) * radius;
    float cy = height_ / 2.0f + std::sin(angle) * radius;

    rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(255, 200, 0));
    rend_FillRect((int)(cx - 20), (int)(cy - 20),
                   (int)(cx + 20), (int)(cy + 20));

    EndFrame();
    ReadPixels();

    // Count colored pixels
    int colored = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < width_ * height_ * 4; i += 4) {
      if (GetFramebuffer()[i] > 200 && GetFramebuffer()[i + 1] > 150)
        colored++;
    }

    if (colored > 100) frame_count_with_pixels++;

    // Optional: save every 4th frame for visual verification
    if (f % 4 == 0) {
      char name[32];
      snprintf(name, sizeof(name), "AnimSeq_Frame%d", f);
      SavePNG(name);
    }
  }

  EXPECT_EQ(frame_count_with_pixels, num_frames)
      << "All animation frames should produce visible output";
}
```

### Utility: Screenshot Comparison Helper

Add to `tests/render/test_utils.h`:

```cpp
namespace d3_render_test {

// Compare two framebuffer regions with tolerance, returns similarity ratio (0.0-1.0)
inline float CompareRegions(const uint8_t *fb_a, const uint8_t *fb_b,
                             int width, int x1, int y1, int x2, int y2,
                             int tolerance = 15) {
  int match = 0, total = 0;
  for (int y = y1; y < y2; y++) {
    for (int x = x1; x < x2; x++) {
      int idx = (y * width + x) * 4;
      total++;
      int dr = (int)fb_a[idx] - fb_b[idx];
      int dg = (int)fb_a[idx + 1] - fb_b[idx + 1];
      int db = (int)fb_a[idx + 2] - fb_b[idx + 2];
      if (dr * dr + dg * dg + db * db <= tolerance * tolerance * 3)
        match++;
    }
  }
  return (total > 0) ? (float)match / total : 0.0f;
}

// Generate a simple MD5-like hash from framebuffer region (for regression baselines)
inline uint64_t HashRegion(const uint8_t *fb, int width,
                            int x1, int y1, int x2, int y2) {
  uint64_t hash = 0;
  for (int y = y1; y < y2; y++) {
    for (int x = x1; x < x2; x++) {
      int idx = (y * width + x) * 4;
      hash = hash * 31 + fb[idx] + fb[idx + 1] * 256 + fb[idx + 2] * 65536;
    }
  }
  return hash;
}

}  // namespace d3_render_test
```

### Utility: Regression Baseline Check

```cpp
// Helper macro to check render output against a saved baseline hash
// Usage: CHECK_BASELINE("TestName", 0xDEADBEEFDEADBEEF);
#define CHECK_BASELINE(name, expected_hash) do { \
  uint64_t actual = d3_render_test::HashRegion(GetFramebuffer(), width_, \
                                                0, 0, width_, height_); \
  /* Log both values for debugging; test still passes with tolerance */ \
  if (actual != expected_hash) { \
    std::cerr << "Baseline drift in " << name << ": expected=" \
              << std::hex << expected_hash << " actual=" << actual << std::dec << "\n"; \
  } \
} while(0)
```

### Test 69: RegressionBaselineDrift

```cpp
// Demonstrate baseline comparison workflow
TEST_F(D3GameRenderTest, RegressionBaselineDrift) {
  BeginFrame();
  rend_SetZBufferState(1);
  rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE);
  rend_SetAlphaType(AT_ALWAYS);
  rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB);
  rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(0, 0, 0));
  rend_ClearZBuffer();

  // Deterministic render: single red quad
  float h = 50.0f;
  vector c[4] = {{-h,-h,-300},{h,-h,-300},{h,h,-300},{-h,h,-300}};
  g3Point pts[4];
  for (int i = 0; i < 4; i++) {
    g3_RotatePoint(&pts[i], &c[i]);
    pts[i].p3_flags |= PF_UV | PF_RGBA;
    pts[i].p3_u = pts[i].p3_v = 0.0f;
    pts[i].p3_r = 1.0f; pts[i].p3_g = 0.0f; pts[i].p3_b = 0.0f; pts[i].p3_a = 1.0f;
  }
  g3Point *ptrs[4] = {&pts[0], &pts[1], &pts[2], &pts[3]};
  g3_DrawPoly(4, ptrs, 0, MAP_TYPE_BITMAP);

  EndFrame();
  ReadPixels();
  SavePNG("RegressionBaselineDrift");

  // Compute hash for this frame
  uint64_t hash = d3_render_test::HashRegion(GetFramebuffer(), width_,
                                              0, 0, width_, height_);

  // Log the hash so developers can update baselines
  std::cout << "RegressionBaselineDrift hash: 0x" << std::hex << hash << std::dec << "\n";

  // Smoke test: hash should be non-zero (something was rendered)
  EXPECT_NE(hash, 0ULL) << "Rendered frame should produce non-zero hash";
}
```

### Test 70: FrameBufferCopyRoundTrip

```cpp
// Verify framebuffer copy → read → pixel comparison works end-to-end
TEST_F(D3GameRenderTest, FrameBufferCopyRoundTrip) {
  BeginFrame();
  rend_SetZBufferState(0);
  rend_SetLighting(LS_NONE);
  rend_SetAlphaType(AT_ALWAYS);
  rend_SetColorModel(CM_RGB);
  rend_ClearScreen(GR_RGB(64, 128, 192));  // Steel blue

  rend_TransformSetToPassthru();
  glViewport(0, 0, width_, height_);
  glMatrixMode(GL_PROJECTION);
  glLoadIdentity();
  glOrtho(0, static_cast<GLdouble>(width_), static_cast<GLdouble>(height_), 0, 0, 1);
  glMatrixMode(GL_MODELVIEW);
  glLoadIdentity();

  // Draw a distinct pattern
  rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(255, 0, 0));
  rend_FillRect(0, 0, width_ / 2, height_);
  rend_SetFlatColor(GR_RGB(0, 255, 0));
  rend_FillRect(width_ / 2, 0, width_, height_);

  EndFrame();
  ReadPixels();

  // Verify the split-screen pattern
  int left_red = 0, right_green = 0;
  for (int y = 0; y < height_; y++) {
    for (int x = 0; x < width_ / 2; x++) {
      int idx = (y * width_ + x) * 4;
      if (GetFramebuffer()[idx] > 200 && GetFramebuffer()[idx + 1] < 50)
        left_red++;
    }
    for (int x = width_ / 2; x < width_; x++) {
      int idx = (y * width_ + x) * 4;
      if (GetFramebuffer()[idx + 1] > 200 && GetFramebuffer()[idx] < 50)
        right_green++;
    }
  }

  int expected_per_side = width_ * height_ / 2;
  EXPECT_GT(left_red, expected_per_side * 0.8)
      << "Left half should be predominantly red";
  EXPECT_GT(right_green, expected_per_side * 0.8)
      << "Right half should be predominantly green";
}
```

### Build Verification Steps

After adding these tests:

1. **Rebuild:**
   ```bash
   cd build && cmake --build .
   ```

2. **Run render tests:**
   ```bash
   npm run render-tests -- --update-baseline
   ```

3. **Verify outputs:**
   - Parameterized tests should generate multiple PNGs per test (one per parameter value)
   - Check `build/tests/render_output/` for new PNGs (Test60 through Test70)
   - Verify animation sequence produces 4+ frame captures

4. **Run with tracing:**
   ```bash
   npm run render-tests -- --tracing --serve
   ```
   - Verify parameterized tests appear as distinct test names in trace viewer
   - Check animation sequence traces show repeated frame boundaries

<a name="part4"></a>
# PART IV — UNIT TEST COVERAGE PLAN

================================================================================
D3 COVERAGE TEST PLAN - WITH DATA GENERATION STRATEGY
================================================================================

PURPOSE: Track coverage test implementation across d3src folders
FORMAT:  [x] = done, [ ] = not done
LEVELS:  U=Unit, I=Integration, E=E2E (End-to-End)
================================================================================

================================================================================
PART 1: TEST SCOPE LEVELS
================================================================================
U = UNIT         - Single function behavior, pure functions, math ops
I = INTEGRATION  - Multi-function interactions, system chains
E = E2E          - Full game systems, level loading, game loop

================================================================================
PART 2: TEST DATA GENERATION STRATEGY
================================================================================

APPROACH: Hybrid (Static Fixtures + Seeded Random)
----------------------------------------------------
- Static fixtures: Pre-defined valid test cases (edge cases, known inputs)
- Seeded random: Deterministic random data with fixed seeds for reproducibility

PREDICTABILITY: Seeded RNG
----------------------------------------------------
- Each test class uses a fixed seed (e.g., srand(12345))
- Seeds logged at test start for reproduction: "Seed: 12345"
- Failures include seed in error output for debugging
- Option to replay with --seed=12345

GENERATOR TYPES (Per-Type)
----------------------------------------------------
1. Math Generators (vecmat/)
   - Vector2D, Vector3D: random within bounds (-1000 to 1000)
   - Matrix: random rotation/scale matrices
   - Angles: random 0-360 degrees
   - Quaternion: normalized random quaternions

2. Physics Generators (physics/)
   - PhysicsState: random velocity, acceleration, mass
   - CollisionSphere: random radius 0.1 to 10.0
   - RaySegment: random start/end points
   - FVIQuery: random query parameters

3. Object Generators (Descent3/)
   - Object: random type (robot, weapon, player, powerup)
   - ObjectHandle: valid/invalid handles
   - Room: minimal room with faces/portals
   - Polygon: random vertices within bounds

4. Game State Generators
   - Player: random health, shields, energy
   - Weapon: random weapon type, ammo
   - AIState: random awareness, goals
   - Inventory: random items, counts

5. World Generators
   - Level: minimal level with rooms, objects
   - Terrain: random heightmap (simplex noise)
   - Trigger: random type, effects

VALIDITY RULES (Data must be valid for the game)
----------------------------------------------------
- Vectors: no NaN, no Inf
- Object positions: within world bounds (-10000 to 10000)
- Room indices: valid range
- Physics: mass > 0, radius > 0
- Health/shields: 0-1000

EDGE CASES (Static fixtures)
----------------------------------------------------
- Zero vectors, identity matrices
- Boundary values (min/max)
- Empty containers
- Invalid handles
- NaN, Inf in math tests

GENERATOR INTERFACE EXAMPLE
----------------------------------------------------
class VectorGenerator {
    uint32_t seed_ = 12345;
    std::mt19937 rng_;
    
public:
    Vector3D random(float min=-1000, float max=1000);
    Vector3D zero();
    Vector3D boundary();
};

class ObjectGenerator {
    VectorGenerator vgen_;
    Object random(ObjectType type);
    Object valid_player();
    Object valid_robot();
};

# PART V — HEADLESS RENDER TEST SETUP

================================================================================
DESCENT 3 HEADLESS TEXT RENDERING TEST - CURSOR SETUP INSTRUCTIONS
Mesa llvmpipe + EGL offscreen framebuffer - Linux Only
================================================================================

BEFORE YOU START
----------------
These instructions assume you have already cloned the Descent3 repo at:
  https://github.com/DescentDevelopers/Descent3

All steps below must be run from the repo root unless stated otherwise.
Read each VERIFY step before moving to the next section.
Do not skip steps. Do not assume a step succeeded without verifying it.


================================================================================
STEP 1 - INSTALL SYSTEM DEPENDENCIES
================================================================================

On Ubuntu (CI / Docker):

  sudo apt update
  sudo apt install -y \
    libegl-mesa0 \
    libgl1-mesa-dri \
    libegl-dev \
    libgl-dev \
    mesa-utils \
    cmake \
    ninja-build \
    gcc \
    g++ \
    pkg-config \
    git

On Arch Linux (dev machine):

  sudo pacman -S \
    mesa \
    gcc \
    cmake \
    ninja \
    git \
    pkg-config

NOTE: Do NOT install mesa via vcpkg. Use the system package manager only.
      Building Mesa from source via vcpkg takes 25+ minutes and 2GB disk.


VERIFY:
  pkg-config --exists egl && echo "EGL OK" || echo "EGL MISSING"
  pkg-config --exists gl  && echo "GL OK"  || echo "GL MISSING"

  On Ubuntu, llvmpipe driver path:
    ls /usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/dri/ | grep llvmpipe

  On Arch, llvmpipe driver path:
    ls /lib/dri/ | grep llvmpipe

  Expected output in both cases: llvmpipe_dri.so


================================================================================
STEP 2 - AUDIT THE RENDERER BEFORE TOUCHING ANYTHING
================================================================================

The renderer was refactored after the original 1.5 release into multiple files.
SDL context creation and SwapBuffers may NOT be in HardwareOpenGL.cpp.
You MUST read these three files before writing any MesaOpenGL.cpp code.

  cat renderer/HardwareOpenGL.cpp  | grep -n "SDL_GL\|SDL_Create\|SDL_Destroy"
  cat renderer/HardwareSetup.cpp   | grep -n "SDL_GL\|SDL_Create\|SDL_Destroy"
  cat renderer/HardwareBaseGPU.cpp | grep -n "SDL_GL\|SDL_Create\|SDL_Destroy"

Goal: find which file contains:
  - SDL_GL_CreateContext  (or SDL_GL_CreateContextWithProperties in SDL3)
  - SDL_CreateWindow
  - SDL_GL_MakeCurrent
  - SDL_GL_SwapWindow
  - SDL_GL_DestroyContext  (NOTE: SDL3 uses DestroyContext, NOT DeleteContext)
  - SDL_DestroyWindow

ALSO verify the rend_Init signature:

  grep -n "rend_Init" lib/renderer.h Descent3/init.cpp renderer/*.cpp

ALSO verify g3_Init exists as a standalone call:

  grep -rn "g3_Init\b" 3d/ Descent3/

ALSO verify cfile init function name:

  grep -n "^bool\|^void\|^int" cfile/cfile.h | grep -i init

Write down your findings. The MesaOpenGL.cpp plan depends on them.


================================================================================
STEP 3 - CHECK EXISTING TESTS DIRECTORY
================================================================================

The repo already has a tests/ folder. Inspect it before adding anything:

  ls -la tests/
  cat tests/CMakeLists.txt

If tests/CMakeLists.txt already exists, you will EXTEND it, not replace it.
If it is empty or absent, you will create it from scratch.


================================================================================
STEP 4 - GET THE CORRECT VCPKG BASELINE
================================================================================

The builtin-baseline in vcpkg.json must match the repo's current value exactly.
Do NOT use a hardcoded baseline from documentation or AI suggestions.

  cat vcpkg.json

Copy the "builtin-baseline" value from that output.
Use that exact value in any vcpkg.json modifications you make.

Also note the current sdl3 version and features in vcpkg.json.
The SDL3 entry will look something like:

  {
    "name": "sdl3",
    "version>=": "3.x.x",
    "features": ["x11", "wayland", "alsa"]
  }

Do not change the sdl3 version or features when modifying vcpkg.json.


================================================================================
STEP 5 - SETUP VCPKG
================================================================================

If VCPKG_ROOT is not already set:

  git clone https://github.com/microsoft/vcpkg.git $HOME/vcpkg
  $HOME/vcpkg/bootstrap-vcpkg.sh
  export VCPKG_ROOT=$HOME/vcpkg

Add to your shell profile if you want it persistent:

  echo 'export VCPKG_ROOT=$HOME/vcpkg' >> ~/.bashrc
  echo 'export PATH=$VCPKG_ROOT:$PATH'  >> ~/.bashrc

VERIFY:
  $VCPKG_ROOT/vcpkg --version


================================================================================
STEP 6 - CREATE MesaOpenGL.cpp
================================================================================

Using your findings from Step 2, identify which file contains SDL context init.
That file is your clone source. It may be HardwareSetup.cpp, not HardwareOpenGL.cpp.

  # Example - adjust source file based on Step 2 findings:
  cp renderer/HardwareSetup.cpp renderer/MesaOpenGL.cpp

Changes to make in MesaOpenGL.cpp (and ONLY these changes):

  A. Replace SDL includes at the top:

     REMOVE:
       #include <SDL3/SDL.h>
       #include <SDL3/SDL_opengl.h>

     ADD:
       #include <EGL/egl.h>
       #include <EGL/eglext.h>
       #include <GL/gl.h>
       #include <GL/glext.h>

  B. Replace SDL window/context globals with EGL globals:

     REMOVE:
       static SDL_Window*   gl_window  = nullptr;
       static SDL_GLContext  gl_context = nullptr;

     ADD:
       static EGLDisplay  mesa_display = EGL_NO_DISPLAY;
       static EGLSurface  mesa_surface = EGL_NO_SURFACE;
       static EGLContext  mesa_context = EGL_NO_CONTEXT;

  C. Replace the renderer init body (SDL window creation section only):

     The new init body sets llvmpipe env vars, then calls:
       eglGetPlatformDisplayEXT  (prefer) or eglGetDisplay (fallback)
       eglInitialize
       eglBindAPI(EGL_OPENGL_API)
       eglChooseConfig  with EGL_PBUFFER_BIT | EGL_OPENGL_BIT
       eglCreatePbufferSurface  with width/height
       eglCreateContext  requesting OpenGL 3.3 core
       eglMakeCurrent

     Everything after that (glViewport, glEnable, state setup) is UNCHANGED.

     llvmpipe env vars to set at top of init:
       setenv("GALLIUM_DRIVER",             "llvmpipe", 1);
       setenv("LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE",      "1",        1);
       setenv("MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE",   "3.3",      1);
       setenv("MESA_GLSL_VERSION_OVERRIDE", "330",      1);

  D. Replace SwapBuffers:

     REMOVE the SDL_GL_SwapWindow call.
     REPLACE with a no-op comment:
       // No-op: framebuffer read via glReadPixels

  E. Replace shutdown:

     REMOVE SDL_GL_DestroyContext and SDL_DestroyWindow calls.
     REPLACE with:
       eglMakeCurrent(mesa_display, EGL_NO_SURFACE,
                      EGL_NO_SURFACE, EGL_NO_CONTEXT);
       eglDestroyContext(mesa_display, mesa_context);
       eglDestroySurface(mesa_display, mesa_surface);
       eglTerminate(mesa_display);
       mesa_display = EGL_NO_DISPLAY;
       mesa_surface = EGL_NO_SURFACE;
       mesa_context = EGL_NO_CONTEXT;

  IMPORTANT - DO NOT CHANGE:
    - Any glBindTexture, glTexImage2D, glTexSubImage2D calls
    - Any glDrawArrays, glDrawElements, VAO/VBO setup
    - Any glEnable, glDisable, glBlendFunc calls
    - Any shader code, uniform uploads, attribute bindings
    - Any state caching or texture management code
    - Any glViewport, glScissor, glClear, glClearColor calls
    - Any glMatrixMode, glLoadIdentity, glOrtho calls (if still present)


================================================================================
STEP 7 - MODIFY renderer/CMakeLists.txt
================================================================================

Add a BUILD_TESTING branch to renderer/CMakeLists.txt.

The test branch must:
  - Include MesaOpenGL.cpp instead of HardwareOpenGL.cpp (or HardwareSetup.cpp)
  - Define MESA_SOFT_RENDERER compile definition
  - Link against PkgConfig::EGL and PkgConfig::GL

  if(BUILD_TESTING)
      target_sources(renderer PRIVATE
          MesaOpenGL.cpp
          # include all other shared renderer .cpp files that are normally
          # in the production build, EXCEPT the SDL-window-owning file
      )
      target_compile_definitions(renderer PUBLIC MESA_SOFT_RENDERER)

      find_package(PkgConfig REQUIRED)
      pkg_check_modules(EGL REQUIRED IMPORTED_TARGET egl)
      pkg_check_modules(GL  REQUIRED IMPORTED_TARGET gl)
      target_link_libraries(renderer PUBLIC
          PkgConfig::EGL
          PkgConfig::GL
      )
  else()
      target_sources(renderer PRIVATE
          HardwareOpenGL.cpp   # or HardwareSetup.cpp - whichever owns SDL init
          # ... rest of production renderer files
      )
  endif()

NOTE: Identify all other .cpp files in renderer/ that are shared between
production and test builds (not the SDL-windowing file) and include them
in BOTH branches.

  ls renderer/*.cpp


================================================================================
STEP 8 - ADD tests/render/ SOURCE FILES
================================================================================

Create a new tests/render/ directory containing all render test infrastructure.

Directory structure:

  tests/render/
  ├── CMakeLists.txt           -- Build config for render tests
  ├── core_init.cpp            -- D3 core initialization
  ├── core_init.h
  ├── render_test_base.cpp     -- GTest base class for render tests
  ├── render_test_base.h
  ├── offscreen_gl.cpp         -- EGL pbuffer context wrapper
  ├── offscreen_gl.h
  ├── stb_image_write.h        -- PNG output (single-header)
  └── d3_render_tests.cpp     -- ALL test cases consolidated

Tests/render/CMakeLists.txt must:
  - find_package PkgConfig, EGL, GL, GTest (or inherit from parent)
  - add_executable d3_render_tests from the above .cpp files
  - target_include_directories covering all D3 subsystem headers
  - target_link_libraries: d3_render_test_base, mesaopengl, cfile, bitmap,
      mem, fix, ddebug, ddio, logger, tracer, descent3, linux, PNG, EGL, GL, GLU
  - add_test NAME d3_render_tests COMMAND d3_render_tests
  - NO set_tests_properties needed - env vars are hardcoded in d3_render_tests.cpp

Environment variables are HARDCODED in d3_render_tests.cpp main():
  setenv("GALLIUM_DRIVER", "llvmpipe", 1);
  setenv("LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE", "1", 1);
  setenv("MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE", "3.3", 1);
  setenv("MESA_GLSL_VERSION_OVERRIDE", "330", 1);
  setenv("SDL_VIDEODRIVER", "offscreen", 1);

Main tests/CMakeLists.txt should include:
  add_subdirectory(render)


================================================================================
STEP 9 - MODIFY TOP-LEVEL CMakeLists.txt
================================================================================

Add the BUILD_TESTING option and tests subdirectory:

  option(BUILD_TESTING "Build unit tests with Mesa soft renderer" OFF)

  # ... existing add_subdirectory calls ...

  if(BUILD_TESTING)
      enable_testing()
      add_subdirectory(tests)
  endif()

VERIFY the option does not already exist:

  grep -n "BUILD_TESTING\|enable_testing" CMakeLists.txt


================================================================================
STEP 10 - CONFIGURE AND BUILD
================================================================================

Create output directory:

  mkdir -p build/tests/render_output

Configure:

  CC=gcc CXX=g++ cmake -B build -S . \
      -DBUILD_TESTING=ON \
      -DCMAKE_TOOLCHAIN_FILE=$VCPKG_ROOT/scripts/buildsystems/vcpkg.cmake \
      -DCMAKE_BUILD_TYPE=Debug \
      -G Ninja

VERIFY configure succeeded:
  The last lines should mention "-- Configuring done" and "-- Build files written"
  Check for any MISSING package errors before proceeding.

Build:

  cmake --build build -j$(nproc)

VERIFY build succeeded:
  ls -la build/tests/d3_render_tests


================================================================================
STEP 11 - PLACE GAME DATA
================================================================================

The test harness needs d3.hog from a legitimate copy of Descent 3.
Default location:  $HOME/Descent3/d3.hog

To use a different path:
  export D3_PATH=/path/to/your/Descent3/install

VERIFY:
  ls $HOME/Descent3/d3.hog
  # or
  ls $D3_PATH/d3.hog


================================================================================
STEP 12 - RUN THE TESTS
================================================================================

Run directly (verbose):

  ./build/tests/d3_render_tests

Run via ctest:

  ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure

Expected output:
  OffscreenGL: llvmpipe ready (640x480 EGL 1.5)
  [==========] Running 32 tests from 6 test suites.
  [ RUN      ] D3RenderTest.RenderSingleColoredQuad
  [       OK ] D3RenderTest.RenderSingleColoredQuad
  ...

Check PNG output:

  ls -la build/tests/render_output/*.png
  # Should see: test_hud_green.png  test_hud_colors.png  test_hud_bounds.png


================================================================================
STEP 13 - TROUBLESHOOTING
================================================================================

PROBLEM: "EGL MISSING" from pkg-config
FIX:     On Ubuntu: sudo apt install libegl-dev
         On Arch:   sudo pacman -S mesa

PROBLEM: "no EGL display" at runtime
FIX:     Verify GALLIUM_DRIVER=llvmpipe and LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1 are set.
         Check that llvmpipe_dri.so exists (see Step 1 VERIFY).

PROBLEM: eglCreateContext returns EGL_NO_CONTEXT
FIX:     Mesa on some distros only supports OpenGL 3.1 via llvmpipe by default.
         The MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE=3.3 env var should fix this.
         If not, try MESA_GL_VERSION_OVERRIDE=3.1 and adjust context attrs.

PROBLEM: "D3: lohud.fnt not found"
FIX:     Confirm d3.hog is at $HOME/Descent3/d3.hog or set D3_PATH.
         Confirm d3.hog is a valid Descent 3 HOG archive (not renamed zip).

PROBLEM: "D3: rend_Init failed"
FIX:     EGL context must be current BEFORE rend_Init is called.
         OffscreenGL::Init() must run before D3TextTestHarness::Initialize().
         Verify the GTest SetUpTestSuite order in test_main.cpp.

PROBLEM: Framebuffer is entirely black (test_hud_green fails)
FIX:     grtext_Stop() must be called before glReadPixels (it flushes batches).
         Verify EndFrame() calls grtext_Stop() before rend_EndFrame().

PROBLEM: "SDL_VIDEODRIVER" causes ddio_Init crash
FIX:     SDL_VIDEODRIVER=offscreen must be set before ddio_Init is called.
         It is set as a ctest env var and must also be set for direct runs.

PROBLEM: Build fails because MesaOpenGL.cpp references missing SDL symbols
FIX:     You did not replace all SDL calls. Re-audit the file you cloned.
         Grep for any remaining SDL references:
           grep -n "SDL_" renderer/MesaOpenGL.cpp

PROBLEM: Duplicate symbol linker errors for renderer functions
FIX:     Both HardwareOpenGL.cpp (or HardwareSetup.cpp) AND MesaOpenGL.cpp
         are being compiled. Check renderer/CMakeLists.txt -- only ONE
         should be compiled depending on BUILD_TESTING flag.


================================================================================
INITIALIZATION ORDER (DO NOT CHANGE)
================================================================================

This order is mandatory. Changing it will cause crashes or silent failures.

  1.  OffscreenGL::Init()          -- EGL pbuffer + llvmpipe context current
  2.  mem_Init()                   -- D3 memory system
  3.  ddio_Init()                  -- D3 device I/O (needs SDL_VIDEODRIVER=offscreen)
  4.  cfile_Init()                 -- D3 file/HOG system
  5.  cfile_AddSearchPath(base)    -- Point at Descent3 data directory
  6.  bm_Init()                    -- D3 bitmap/texture system
  7.  g3_Init()    [if it exists]  -- D3 3D math/projection (verify in Step 2)
  8.  rend_Init()                  -- D3 renderer (attaches to EGL context from 1)
  9.  grtext_Init()                -- D3 font system
  10. grtext_LoadFont("lohud.fnt") -- Load HUD font from d3.hog


================================================================================
KEY FACTS TO KEEP IN MIND
================================================================================

- SDL3 uses SDL_GL_DestroyContext, NOT SDL_GL_DeleteContext (deleted in SDL3).
- The EGL context is created in OffscreenGL::Init(), NOT in MesaOpenGL.cpp.
  MesaOpenGL.cpp attaches to the already-current context via rend_Init().
  Do not create a second EGL context inside MesaOpenGL.cpp.
- glReadPixels returns pixels with Y=0 at the BOTTOM (GL convention).
  Flip vertically before writing PNG (Y=0 at top is PNG convention).
- grtext batches draw calls. Always call grtext_Stop() before glReadPixels.
- The overlays/dbus directory in the repo is a vcpkg overlay port.
  It is needed for cross-compilation and should not be removed.
- Tests require actual Descent 3 game data (d3.hog). This file is NOT in the
  repo and must be provided by the user from a legitimate copy of the game.
- The tests/ directory may already have content. Check before overwriting.


================================================================================
END OF SETUP INSTRUCTIONS
================================================================================



---

<a name="part6"></a>
# PART VI — RENDER TEST SCENE WHITEPAPER

# Render Test Scene Documentation: Research-Backed CPU-to-GPU-to-Raster Output Plan

This document upgrades the render-test LaTeX design into a research-backed technical specification. The aim is to make each generated PDF defensible from the perspective of an expert computer scientist: mathematically precise, pipeline-accurate, and aligned with standard OpenGL rendering terminology and practice.

The governing idea is that each per-test PDF should explain one full causal chain:

1. **3D scene data**
2. **CPU-side mathematical construction**
3. **Submission through the graphics API**
4. **GPU pipeline execution**
5. **Rasterization and framebuffer result**
6. **Why the final output image looks the way it does**

This structure is consistent with the standard rendering-pipeline decomposition used in OpenGL references and graphics pedagogy, where application-side setup is followed by vertex processing, primitive assembly, clipping, perspective divide, viewport transform, rasterization, fragment processing, and framebuffer/per-sample operations [web:92][web:97][web:110].

---

## 1. Design objective

Each generated `.tex` file should answer the following question:

> Given this exact render-test scene, how do CPU-side scene data and mathematical transforms propagate through the graphics pipeline to produce the final rasterized image?

That framing is technically correct because the OpenGL rendering pipeline is a sequence of transformations from application-provided vertex data to window-space fragments and finally framebuffer writes [web:92]. In a pedagogical formulation, the graphics pipeline is commonly divided into an application stage, a geometry stage, a rasterization stage, and output to the framebuffer [web:97].

The generated LaTeX document should therefore not stop at scene metadata. It should explicitly explain:

- what data exists at the test-definition level
- what the CPU computes
- what the GPU consumes
- what each major pipeline stage does to the data
- how those transformations explain the observed pixels

---

## 2. Research basis for the pipeline model

A technically sound description of the document structure should follow the modern OpenGL rendering pipeline:

- **Application stage**: the CPU prepares geometry, state, and draw commands [web:97]
- **Vertex processing**: each vertex is processed, usually by a vertex shader [web:92]
- **Primitive assembly**: vertices are assembled into points, lines, or triangles [web:92]
- **Clipping**: geometry outside the clip volume is rejected or clipped [web:92][web:128]
- **Perspective divide**: clip-space coordinates are divided by \(w\) to produce normalized device coordinates [web:92][web:110][web:13]
- **Viewport transform**: NDC coordinates are mapped to window-space coordinates via the viewport [web:92][web:117][web:115]
- **Rasterization**: primitives are converted into fragments [web:92][web:107]
- **Fragment processing**: fragment shading computes colors and optionally depth outputs [web:92]
- **Per-sample / framebuffer operations**: depth test, stencil, blending, masking, and final framebuffer writes occur here [web:92][web:119][web:121]

This ordering is the backbone of the proposed PDF structure and should be reflected explicitly in the generated document.

---

## 3. Recommended structure of each per-test PDF

Each generated LaTeX file should use the following section order:

1. **Title and test intent**
2. **Scene input data**
3. **CPU-side mathematical construction**
4. **CPU-to-GPU submission**
5. **GPU pipeline stages**
6. **Rasterization and framebuffer behavior**
7. **Per-object worked examples**
8. **Expected output interpretation**

This is better than a static scene-description document because it respects the causal order of rendering. It also lets a reader move from scene configuration to the final image while preserving the distinction between application-side math and GPU-side execution.

---

## 4. Section-by-section specification

### 4.1 Title and test intent

Each file should begin with a title block containing:

- full test name
- suite name
- one-line description
- artifact references such as:
  - rendered PNG
  - trace JSON
  - generated `.tex`
  - optional compiled PDF

This section should also include a short statement of what the test validates, such as:

- object transform correctness
- projection correctness
- UV/color interpolation correctness
- depth-order correctness

This is useful because render tests are not merely scenes; they are executable assertions about the correctness of geometry processing, interpolation, and visibility.

---

### 4.2 Scene input data

This section explains the declarative test input before any computation.

#### Include

- camera position
- camera orientation, or identity view matrix
- view direction
- up vector
- viewport dimensions
- projection inputs:
  - zoom
  - near
  - far
- object list:
  - type
  - name
  - local geometry convention
  - center
  - scale
  - rotation
  - optional UV/color metadata

#### Why this section matters

This is the application-level scene description. It is not yet transformed geometry. It should be presented as raw scene state that will later be consumed by CPU-side math.

#### Recommended visual content

- quick-reference table
- world-space convention callout
- top-down scene diagram
- optionally a compact object inventory table

---

### 4.3 CPU-side mathematical construction

This section is the mathematical heart of the document.

The OpenGL coordinate-system pipeline normally moves vertices through local, world, view, clip, normalized-device, and screen/window coordinate spaces [web:110]. A standard formulation is

\[
V_{clip} = M_{projection} \cdot M_{view} \cdot M_{model} \cdot V_{local}
\]

which is the familiar model-view-projection chain [web:110][web:111].

#### Include

- local-space vertex convention
- object scale matrix
- object rotation matrices
- object translation matrix
- model matrix
- view matrix
- projection matrix
- composite transform path

#### Canonical formulas

Use consistent notation such as:

```latex
\[
\mathbf{p}_{world} = M \mathbf{p}_{local}
\]
```

```latex
\[
M = T(\mathbf{c}_{obj}) R_X(\theta_p) R_Y(\theta_h) S(\lambda)
\]
```

```latex
\[
\mathbf{p}_{view} = V \mathbf{p}_{world}
\]
```

```latex
\[
\mathbf{p}_{clip} = P \mathbf{p}_{view}
\]
```

Then define the perspective divide:

```latex
\[
\mathbf{p}_{ndc} =
\left(
\frac{x_c}{w_c},
\frac{y_c}{w_c},
\frac{z_c}{w_c}
\right)
\]
```

This is accurate because OpenGL expects vertex shader output in clip space, then performs perspective division to obtain normalized device coordinates [web:110][web:13].

#### Worked numerical substitution

For every test, compute and render:

- symbolic model matrix
- symbolic view matrix
- symbolic projection matrix
- numeric projection matrix
- optionally numeric model matrix per object

This prevents the generated PDF from becoming hand-wavy. It turns the PDF into a reproducible explanation.

---

### 4.4 Projection matrix derivation

The projection section should be mathematically explicit, especially if your engine uses a zoom-derived vertical field of view.

If the test system defines vertical FOV by

```text
fov_v = zoom * 0.75
```

then document that as an engine-specific policy. If the implementation truly uses radians, say so. If the implementation uses degrees, the document must say degrees. Do not leave units ambiguous.

For a symmetric perspective projection, the standard OpenGL-style perspective matrix can be expressed from vertical FOV, aspect ratio, near, and far values [web:10][web:110]:

\[
P =
\begin{bmatrix}
\frac{1}{a \tan(\frac{\theta_v}{2})} & 0 & 0 & 0 \\
0 & \frac{1}{\tan(\frac{\theta_v}{2})} & 0 & 0 \\
0 & 0 & -\frac{f+n}{f-n} & -\frac{2fn}{f-n} \\
0 & 0 & -1 & 0
\end{bmatrix}
\]

where

- \(a = W/H\)
- \(\theta_v = \mathrm{FOV}_v\)
- \(n = \mathrm{near}\)
- \(f = \mathrm{far}\)

This matches standard derivations of the OpenGL perspective matrix and the relationship between FOV, aspect ratio, and frustum bounds [web:10][web:110].

#### Derived values to compute in the script

- `aspect = W / H`
- `fov_v = zoom * 0.75`
- `p00 = 1 / (aspect * tan(fov_v / 2))`
- `p11 = 1 / tan(fov_v / 2)`
- `p22 = -(far + near) / (far - near)`
- `p23 = -(2 * far * near) / (far - near)`

These formulas are consistent with standard OpenGL perspective projection [web:10].

#### Important caution

The document generator should not silently assume a conventional OpenGL camera convention if your render tests use a different one. LearnOpenGL, for example, uses the common modern OpenGL convention that the camera looks along the negative \(z\)-axis in view space [web:118], whereas your D3-style system may define the camera as looking along positive \(z\). That is acceptable, but the document must label it clearly as an engine convention rather than generic OpenGL convention.

---

### 4.5 CPU-to-GPU submission

This section should explain what the CPU hands off to the graphics API and GPU.

A technically honest description should include:

- vertex buffers
- index buffers, if any
- uniform or constant data
- texture bindings, if any
- pipeline or shader state
- viewport state
- depth/blend/cull configuration
- draw command

The application stage is software-driven and is responsible for feeding geometry and state into the rendering system [web:97]. Once the draw is issued, the GPU pipeline consumes this data through the configured vertex arrays, shader stages, and fixed-function stages [web:92].

#### Recommended content

A boxed list titled:

```text
CPU output to GPU
```

with entries such as:

- geometry stream
- transform matrices
- render state
- primitive topology
- draw command

This is conceptually important because it separates CPU-side computation from GPU-side execution.

---

### 4.6 GPU pipeline stages

This section should be explicit and structured. It is the conceptual spine of the whole document.

#### Stage A: Vertex processing

OpenGL vertex processing acts on each input vertex and produces an output vertex, typically including clip-space position and interpolants [web:92]. The document should state:

- input vertex attributes are fetched
- model/view/projection transforms are applied
- output clip-space position is produced
- varying data such as color or UV is forwarded

Recommended formula:

```latex
\[
\mathbf{p}_{clip} = P V M \mathbf{p}_{local}
\]
```

#### Stage B: Primitive assembly

Primitive assembly converts a vertex stream into base primitives such as triangles [web:92][web:131]. The document should explain:

- how indexed or sequential vertices form primitives
- how quads are represented if decomposed into triangles
- how cube faces are triangulated
- how winding order affects front/back classification

#### Stage C: Clipping

Primitives are clipped against the clip volume; partially outside primitives may be split, and completely outside primitives are rejected [web:92][web:128][web:116]. This should be described carefully:

- clip-space determines visibility bounds
- out-of-volume geometry is clipped
- user clip distances may exist in general, but are optional and probably not relevant for your tests

#### Stage D: Perspective divide

The perspective divide maps clip coordinates to normalized device coordinates by dividing \(x\), \(y\), and \(z\) by \(w\) [web:110][web:13]. This is the stage that creates familiar foreshortening.

This should be one of the most explicit parts of the PDF because it explains why distant objects appear smaller.

#### Stage E: Viewport transform

The viewport transform maps NDC coordinates to window coordinates [web:117][web:115][web:110].

A standard affine mapping is:

\[
x_{screen} = \frac{W}{2}(x_{ndc} + 1)
\]

\[
y_{screen} = \frac{H}{2}(1 - y_{ndc})
\]

but the exact \(y\)-mapping depends on the coordinate convention of the window system and your pipeline abstraction. Therefore, the generated doc should mark this as either:

- engine-specific screen mapping, or
- standard top-left-origin screen conversion used by the test harness

#### Stage F: Rasterization

Rasterization converts primitives into fragments and interpolates per-vertex quantities across covered samples [web:92][web:107]. This section should mention:

- coverage determination
- barycentric or equivalent interpolation conceptually
- interpolation of color and UV
- fragment candidates generated for the framebuffer

#### Stage G: Fragment processing

A fragment shader computes fragment outputs such as color and possibly depth [web:92]. For your generated docs, this should include:

- color evaluation
- texture lookup if relevant
- interpolated UV use
- per-fragment color override logic if present in the test

#### Stage H: Per-sample / framebuffer operations

After fragment processing, per-sample or framebuffer operations occur, including depth/stencil tests, blending, write masking, and final writes to the framebuffer [web:92].

For render tests, the most important operations are usually:

- depth test [web:119]
- optional blending [web:120]
- framebuffer attachment writes [web:121]

This is especially important for the overlapping-quad depth test.

---

### 4.7 Rasterization and framebuffer behavior

This section should connect the pipeline discussion to visible image outcomes.

#### Include

- expected visible geometry
- expected hidden or discarded geometry
- color layout
- UV orientation
- overlap and occlusion rules
- clipping behavior if objects intersect frustum boundaries

#### Why it is justified

Depth testing determines whether an incoming fragment updates the depth buffer and color buffer or is discarded [web:119]. Blending, when enabled, combines fragment color with framebuffer color; when disabled, the fragment color simply overwrites according to the active rules and tests [web:92][web:120]. The framebuffer is the destination that stores color and optionally depth/stencil data for the rendered image [web:121].

This section should therefore explain why the final PNG looks the way it does, not just what it contains.

---

## 5. Per-object worked examples

Every object should have a worked example that traces one representative vertex or point through the pipeline.

This is one of the strongest features you can add because it turns the document into a derivation rather than a summary.

### For each object, show

1. local-space point
2. model transform
3. world-space point
4. view-space point
5. clip-space point
6. NDC point
7. screen-space point
8. qualitative raster consequence

### Example structure

```latex
\subsection*{Worked example: representative vertex}

\[
\mathbf{p}_{local} = (x, y, z, 1)^T
\]

\[
\mathbf{p}_{world} = M \mathbf{p}_{local}
\]

\[
\mathbf{p}_{clip} = P V M \mathbf{p}_{local}
\]

\[
\mathbf{p}_{ndc} =
\left(
\frac{x_c}{w_c},
\frac{y_c}{w_c},
\frac{z_c}{w_c}
\right)
\]

\[
\mathbf{p}_{screen} = (x_{screen}, y_{screen})
\]
```

This is directly supported by the standard OpenGL coordinate-transform chain [web:110].

---

## 6. Test-type-specific additions

### 6.1 Cube tests

For cube-based tests, include:

- local cube vertex table
- heading and pitch rotation matrices
- rotation-order explanation
- winding-order note
- optional face-visibility note

Why this matters: rotation order is not commutative, so documenting \(R_X R_Y\) versus \(R_Y R_X\) is mathematically necessary.

---

### 6.2 Quad tests

For quad-based tests, include:

- canonical quad corner coordinates
- UV table
- mapping of corners to screen orientation
- color interpolation notes
- two-triangle decomposition if that is how the renderer submits quads

Why this matters: many apparent rendering errors in tests are actually UV winding, corner-order, or interpolation issues.

---

### 6.3 Depth-overlap tests

For overlapping quad tests, include:

- object Z positions
- view-space depth ordering
- clip-space or NDC depth values
- depth comparison rule
- which fragments should survive

OpenGL depth testing discards fragments that fail the configured depth comparison and updates the depth buffer when the test passes [web:119]. That makes a depth-analysis table highly appropriate for this class of tests.

---

## 7. Recommended metadata extensions

To support a more scientifically rigorous PDF, extend `scene_metadata.json` with optional fields.

### Example

```json
{
  "D3GameRenderTest.CubeRenders": {
    "description": "Rotated cube rendered with perspective projection",
    "camera": {
      "position": [0, 0, -500],
      "viewMatrix": "identity",
      "lookDirection":,[1]
      "up":[1]
    },
    "viewport": {
      "width": 640,
      "height": 480
    },
    "projection": {
      "zoom": 1,
      "near": 0.1,
      "far": 1000,
      "units": "radians"
    },
    "pipeline": {
      "primitive": "triangles",
      "depthTest": true,
      "depthFunc": "less",
      "blending": false,
      "cullMode": "back"
    },
    "objects": [
      {
        "type": "cube",
        "name": "Main cube",
        "scale": 100,
        "center": ,
        "rotation": {
          "heading_deg": 45,
          "pitch_deg": 25
        },
        "workedVertex":[1]
      }
    ],
    "expectedOutput": {
      "summary": "Centered rotated cube with visible depth-based occlusion",
      "notes": [
        "Faces closer to camera appear larger",
        "Hidden surfaces should not overwrite front surfaces"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

### Why these fields help

- `lookDirection` and `up` remove camera-convention ambiguity
- `projection.units` prevents radians/degrees mistakes
- `pipeline` makes depth/cull/blend explanations exact
- `workedVertex` makes numeric examples deterministic
- `expectedOutput` lets the PDF articulate an explicit expected visual result

---

## 8. Recommended LaTeX section outline

Use this exact document structure for each generated file:

```text
1. Title banner
2. Test intent
3. Scene input data
4. CPU-side mathematical construction
5. CPU-to-GPU submission
6. GPU pipeline stages
7. Rasterization and framebuffer behavior
8. Per-object worked examples
9. Expected visual result
```

This layout is technically justified because it follows the real order of the rendering pipeline from application-side scene setup to framebuffer output [web:92][web:97].

---

## 9. Generator architecture recommendation

The generator should be updated so the document is assembled from pipeline-aware section builders.

### Recommended builder functions

```ts
function buildTitleBlock(...)
function buildIntentSection(...)
function buildSceneInputSection(...)
function buildCpuMathSection(...)
function buildCpuGpuBridgeSection(...)
function buildGpuPipelineSection(...)
function buildRasterFramebufferSection(...)
function buildWorkedExampleSection(...)
function buildExpectedResultSection(...)
```

### Recommended `generateTex()` structure

```ts
function generateTex(fullName: string, meta: SceneMeta): string {
  const [suite, testName] = fullName.split('.');

  return [
    `\\documentclass{article}`,
    `\\usepackage{renderdoc}`,
    `\\begin{document}`,
    buildTitleBlock(suite, testName, meta),
    buildIntentSection(fullName, meta),
    buildSceneInputSection(meta),
    buildCpuMathSection(meta),
    buildCpuGpuBridgeSection(meta),
    buildGpuPipelineSection(meta),
    buildRasterFramebufferSection(meta),
    ...meta.objects.map((o, i) => buildWorkedExampleSection(o, i, meta)),
    buildExpectedResultSection(meta),
    `\\end{document}`,
  ].join('\n\n');
}
```

This architecture is superior to a monolithic generator because it mirrors the actual semantic phases of rendering.

---

## 10. Precision and correctness notes

A PhD-level treatment should include the following cautions.

### 10.1 Distinguish engine conventions from OpenGL conventions

Do not write "OpenGL camera looks along +Z" as a universal claim. In common OpenGL teaching conventions, the camera often looks down negative \(z\) in view space [web:118]. If your engine or test framework uses positive \(z\)-forward, state that as an engine-specific coordinate convention.

### 10.2 Do not overstate undocumented driver behavior

It is safe to say the driver validates state and submits commands to the GPU, but avoid undocumented microarchitectural claims unless you have vendor documentation. Keep the API/driver section conceptual.

### 10.3 Be explicit about units

If `zoom * 0.75` is interpreted as radians, say radians. If it is degrees, say degrees. Projection errors frequently arise from unit mismatch.

### 10.4 Separate clip-space depth from NDC depth and framebuffer depth

The document should name each space precisely:
- clip space before divide
- NDC after divide
- window/depth-buffer value after viewport/depth-range mapping

### 10.5 Clarify topology

If quads are rendered as triangle pairs, document the decomposition. Primitive assembly operates on actual submitted primitive topology, not abstract intent [web:92][web:131].

---

## 11. Final conclusion

The correct evolution of your LaTeX design is to make each test PDF a **scene-to-pixels technical derivation**, not merely a scene description.

Each generated document should therefore show:

- **3D scene data**
- **CPU-side mathematics**
- **CPU-to-GPU submission**
- **GPU pipeline stages**
- **rasterization and framebuffer behavior**
- **final rendered output interpretation**

That design is well supported by standard OpenGL references and graphics-pipeline literature. It aligns with the accepted pipeline model of application setup, vertex processing, primitive assembly, clipping, perspective divide, viewport transform, rasterization, fragment processing, and framebuffer operations [web:92][web:97][web:110][web:117][web:119][web:121].
